<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{definition_DtN_sol_ref_alea}
The Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DtN) operator $\Lambda^k:H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\Sigma_{L}\big)\to H^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\Sigma_{L}\big)$ is defined as
                  
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               
                
           
\begin{equation}\forall \varphi,\psi\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\Sigma_{L}\big), \quad\big\langle\Lambda^k \varphi,\psi \big\rangle_{\Sigma_{L}}= {(2\pi)^{-(d-1)/2}}\displaystyle  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \mathrm{i} \sqrt{k^2-|\boldsymbol{\zeta}|^2}\ \widehat{\varphi}(\boldsymbol{\zeta}) \ \overline{\widehat{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\zeta})} \ \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\end{equation}
where here and in the sequel $\big\langle\cdot,\cdot \big\rangle_{\Sigma_{L}}$ denotes the sesquilinear duality product between $H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_{L})$ and $H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_{L})$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:prop_operateur_DtN_alea}
The operator $\Lambda^k:H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\Sigma_{L}\big)\to H^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\Sigma_{L}\big)$ is a continuous operator such that
\begin{equation}\forall\varphi\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\Sigma_{L}\big),\quad
     {\rm Re}\left[\big\langle\Lambda^k \varphi,\varphi  \big\rangle_{\Sigma_{L}} \right]\leq 0 \quad \text{and}\quad 
     {\rm Im}\left[\big\langle\Lambda^k \varphi , \varphi \big\rangle_{\Sigma_{L}}\right] \geq 0.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
{\cite[Lemma 15]{baden2020existence}}\label{thm:ref_alea}
   Let $\ell_\varepsilon^\omega$ be in $[H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})]'$, \emph{i.e.} a continuous antilinear form on $H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})$. For all $\varepsilon>0$ and $\omega$, there exists a unique $u_\varepsilon^\omega \in H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})$ solution of 
   \begin{equation*}
    \forall v \in H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}),\quad a_\varepsilon^\omega\big(u^{\omega}_\varepsilon,v \big) \ = \ \ell_\varepsilon^\omega(v ),
    \end{equation*}
     where $a_\varepsilon^\omega$ is defined in \eqref{eq:a_epsomega}. Moreover, there exists $C>0$ that depends only on $k$, $L$ and $\gamma$ such that
   \begin{equation}
       \|u^\omega_\varepsilon \|_{H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})} \leq C \| \ell^\omega_\varepsilon\|_{[H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})]'}.
   \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{definition:stationary}$f:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^p$ is said to be stationary with respect to $\tau$ if 
\begin{equation*}\forall \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel,\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}, \quad f^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)=f^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel}\omega}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel),\quad \omega\in\Omega.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
$(\tau_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel})_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}$ is said to be ergodic if any $\tau$-invariant event has probability 0 or 1, \textit{i.e.}
\begin{equation*}\forall A\in\mathcal{F},\quad (\forall \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}, \tau^{-1}_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel} A=A)\implies (\mathbb{P}(A)\in\{0,1\}).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{theorem:Birkhoff}Let $f$ be a stationary process with respect to an ergodic action $(\tau_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel})_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}$ such that $f\in L^p(\Omega, L^1_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}))$. Then almost surely (\textit{a.s.}) and in $L^p(\Omega)$ for any measurable bounded domain $K\subset\mathbb{R}^{d-1}$\vspace{-0.2cm}\begin{equation*}\forall \boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^d,\quad \fint_{K_t}f^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\xrightarrow[t\to \infty]{}\mathbb{E}[f],\end{equation*}where $K_t\coloneqq \left\{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}|t^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in K\right\}$ denotes the homothetic dilation with ratio $t>0$ of $K$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- hypothesis-1: body_md -->
\makeatletter\def(Mix){(H1)}\makeatother
\label{hyp:mixing}There exists $m>2d$ such that \begin{equation*}\sup_{y_d\in[0,h]}\quad \mathbb{E}[R(\cdot, y_d)^m]<+\infty.\end{equation*}
<!-- end hypothesis-1 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:wpue}
Let $\ell_\varepsilon$ be a continuous antilinear form on $L^2\big(\Omega,H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)$ then for all $\varepsilon>0$, there exists a unique $u_\varepsilon \in L^2\big(\Omega,H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)$ such that for a.e. $\omega\in\Omega$, $u_\varepsilon^\omega$ is solution of \eqref{FV_alea_ref}. Moreover, there exists $C>0$ depending only on $k$, $L$ and $\gamma$ such that 
\begin{equation}
\mathbb{E}\left[\|u_\varepsilon^\omega\|^2_{H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})} \right]\ \leq \ C \   \mathbb{E}\Big[\|\ell_\varepsilon\|_{\big[H^1_0(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big]'}^2 \Big].
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Note that $\mathcal{W}_0(D)\not\subset \mathcal{L}^2(D).$
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:Utnwellposed}
Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mixing} then for all $\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F\in\mathcal{L}^2(D)$, $\boldsymbol{G}\in\mathcal{L}^2(D)^n$, $\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\in\mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_0)$ and $\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^N\in \mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_H)$,  Problem \eqref{FV_Dirichlet_pb_releve_borne_regularise} is well posed in $\mathcal{W}_0(D)$. Moreover the unique solution $\widetilde{U}\in\mathcal{W}_0(D)$ verifies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:estUt}
\|\widetilde{U}\|_{\mathcal{W}_0(D)}\lesssim \|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|_{\mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_0)}+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^{N}\|_{\mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_H)}+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F\|_{\mathcal{L}^2(D)}+\|G\|_{\mathcal{L}^2(D)}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
One can show that the unique solution  in $\mathcal{W}_0(D)$ of Problem \eqref{FV_Dirichlet_pb_releve_borne_regularise} satisfies almost surely Problem \eqref{eq:NFtGen} in the sense of distributions. That is why, in what follows, we designate the unique solution of \eqref{eq:NFtGen}  in $\mathcal{W}_0(D)$ the variational solution.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
Assume that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mixing} then $(\mathcal{W}_0(D), \|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{W}_0(D)})$ is a Hilbert space.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
It is easy to see that $\mathcal{W}_0(D)$ is a Pre-Hilbert space. Let us prove completeness. For all $R>0$ and $\omega\in\Omega$, let $D^\omega_R\coloneqq D^\omega\cap(\square_R\times (0,+\infty)).$
Since a.s. $\mu^{-1}\in L^1_{loc}(D^\omega_R)$ , the weighted Sobolev space
\begin{equation*}
W_0(D^\omega_R)\coloneqq \left\{v\in H_{loc}^1\big(D^\omega_R\big), v=0\,\text{on }\partial \mathcal{P}^\omega, \int_{\square_R}\int_0^{+\infty} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ (\mu(\boldsymbol{y})|v(\boldsymbol{y})|^2+|\nabla v(\boldsymbol{y})|^2) \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}<+\infty\right\}
\end{equation*}
equipped with the norm
\begin{equation*}
\forall v\in W_0(D^\omega_R), \quad \|v\|_{W_0(D^\omega_R)}\coloneqq  \int_{\square_R}\int_0^{+\infty} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ (\mu(\boldsymbol{y})|v(\boldsymbol{y})|^2+|\nabla v(\boldsymbol{y})|^2) \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}
\end{equation*}
is a Hilbert space \cite[Theorem 1.11]{kufner1984define}. Therefore $(L^2(\Omega, W_0(D^\omega_R)), \|\cdot\|_{L^2(\Omega, W_0(D^\omega_R))})$ is also a Hilbert space. 

Let $(v_n)_{n\inn\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a Cauchy sequence in $\mathcal{W}_0(D)$. By stationarity $(v_n)_{n\inn\in\mathbb{N}}$ is a Cauchy sequence in  $L^2(\Omega, W_0(D^\omega_R))$ for any $R>0$. As  $\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\uparrow \square_R=\mathbb{R}^{d-1}$, there exists $v\in H^1_{loc}(\mathbb{R}^d,L^2(\Omega))$ such that for all $R>0$, $v_n\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}v$ in $L^2(\Omega, W_0(D^\omega_R))$.

It remains to show that $\mathbb{1}_D v(\cdot,x_d)$ is stationary for all $x_d>0$. Let $n\in\mathbb{N}$. By stationarity of $\mathbb{1}_D v_n(\cdot,x_d)$, we have for a.e. $\omega\in \Omega$, $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times(0,+\infty)$ and $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}$
\begin{equation*}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathbb{1}_{D}v_n^\omega((\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, x_d))-\mathbb{1}_{D}v_n^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel}\omega}(\boldsymbol{x})\right|\right]=0.\end{equation*}
We would like to pass to the limit in this equality but we only have that $v_n\xrightarrow[]{}v$ in $L^2(\Omega, W_0(D^\omega_R))$.
Let $R$ such that $\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel, \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in \square_{R}$. Using \eqref{eq:Eh-1} and the stationarity of $\mu$ we obtain by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
\begin{multline}\label{eq:stati}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_{R}}\int_0^{x_d}\left|\mathbb{1}_{D}v^\omega((\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \tilde{x}_d))-\mathbb{1}_{D}v^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel}\omega}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}})\right|d\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_{R}}\int_0^{x_d}\mu^{-1}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}})d\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}\right]\\
\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_{R}}\int_0^{x_d}\mu^\omega((\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \tilde{x}_d))\left|\mathbb{1}_{D}v_n^\omega((\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \tilde{x}_d))-\mathbb{1}_{D}v^\omega((\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \tilde{x}_d))\right|^2d\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}\right]\right. \\
\left.+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_{R}}\int_0^{x_d}\mu^\omega((\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \tilde{x}_d))\left|\mathbb{1}_{D} v_n^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel}\omega}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}})-\mathbb{1}_{D} v^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel}\omega}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}})\right|^2d\tilde{\boldsymbol{x}}\right]\right).
\end{multline}
Since $v_n\xrightarrow[]{} v$ in $\vspace{0.1cm} L^2(\Omega, W_0(D^\omega_{R}))$, we deduce by passing to the limit in this last inequality that $\mathbb{1}_D v(\cdot,x_d)$ is stationary for all $x_d>0$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:weightedtrace}
Let $\mathcal{P}$ verify Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mixing}. Let $\alpha\in\{0\}\cup[h,+\infty)$. There exists $C_\alpha$ such that for any $v\in \mathcal{W}_0(D)$
\begin{equation*}
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel,\alpha)\left|v^\omega\big|_{{\Sigma_\alpha}}\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\right] \ \leq C_\alpha \ \|v\|_{\mathcal{W}_0(D)}^2.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
We first prove the result for $\alpha\in\{0,h\}$. Let $v\in\mathcal{W}_0(D)$. Let us consider the Vorono{\"\i} diagram $(V_n^\omega)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ in $\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times[0,h]$ associated with the point process $\{\boldsymbol{x}_n^\omega\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ defined by the sets 
\begin{equation*}
  V_n^\omega \coloneqq  \left\{ \boldsymbol{y}\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times[0,h] \text{ such that } \big|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{x}_n^\omega\big| = \min_{j\in \mathbb{N}} \Big|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{x}_j^\omega\Big| \ \right\} , \ \ n\in \mathbb{N} . 
\end{equation*} 
For $\omega\in\Omega, n\in\mathbb{N}$ let $\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)$ denote the intersection of the hyperplane $\{y_d=\alpha\}$ with the Vorono{\"\i} cell $V_n^\omega$ . Let $(\rho, \boldsymbol{\theta})$ be the spherical coordinate system centered at $\boldsymbol{x}_n^\omega$. Since $V_n^\omega$ is convex, $\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)$ can be parametrized as $\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)\coloneqq \left\{(r(\boldsymbol{\theta}), \boldsymbol{\theta}), r(\boldsymbol{\theta})\in [1,+\infty), \boldsymbol{\theta}\in [\boldsymbol{\theta}_0, \boldsymbol{\theta}_1]\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right\}$. Since $v^\omega\in H^1(V_n^\omega)$ with $v^\omega=0$ on $\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}^\omega_n)$, there exists $(v_p^\omega)_{p\in\mathbb{N}}$ in $\mathcal{C}^\infty(V_n^\omega)^\mathbb{N}$ with $v_p^\omega=0$ on $\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}^\omega_n)$  such that $v_p^\omega\to v^\omega$ in $H^1(V_n^\omega)$. We have for all $p$, \begin{equation*}\forall \boldsymbol{\theta}\in [\boldsymbol{\theta}_0, \boldsymbol{\theta}_1],\quad \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}v_p^\omega(r(\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\theta})=\int_1^{r(\boldsymbol{\theta})} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\partial_r v^\omega_p(s,\boldsymbol{\theta}) ds.\end{equation*}
We deduce for any non-negative function $\tilde{\mu}$ on $(1,+\infty)$, for all $\boldsymbol{\theta}\in [\boldsymbol{\theta}_0, \boldsymbol{\theta}_1]$
\begin{equation*}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|v_p^\omega(r(\boldsymbol{\theta}),\boldsymbol{\theta})|^2\tilde{\mu}(r(\boldsymbol{\theta}))r(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{d-1}\leq\int_1^{r(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\partial_r  v_p^\omega(s, \boldsymbol{\theta})|^2s^{d-1}ds\left(\int_1^{r(\boldsymbol{\theta})} s^{1-d}ds\right)\tilde{\mu}(r(\boldsymbol{\theta}))r(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{d-1}.\end{equation*}
For $\tilde{\mu}(\rho)\coloneqq \displaystyle\left(\int_1^{\rho} s^{1-d}ds\right)^{-1}\rho^{1-d}$, we get after integrating with respect to $\boldsymbol{\theta}$
\begin{equation*}\int_{\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\tilde{\mu}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|v_p^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|^2\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\leq\int_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_0}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_1}\int_1^{r(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\partial_r  v_p^\omega(s, \boldsymbol{\theta})|^2\, s^{d-1}\mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}.\end{equation*}
We can now pass to the limit when $p\to+\infty$ to obtain the first inequality of 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wvn}
  \begin{aligned}
  \int_{\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\tilde{\mu}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|v^\omega\big|_{\Sigma_\alpha}|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel&\leq\int_{\boldsymbol{\theta}_0}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_1}\int_1^{r(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\partial_r  v^\omega(s, \boldsymbol{\theta})|^2\, s^{d-1}\mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\theta}\\&\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_n(\alpha)\times(0,h)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}.
  \end{aligned}\end{equation}
                                                                                                                                             
By definition \eqref{eq:h}, for $\boldsymbol{y}\in V_n^\omega$, $\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})=r(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{-m}$ with $m>2d$. Therefore $\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}\mapsto{\mu^\omega}/{\tilde{\mu}^\omega}(\boldsymbol{y})$ is bounded by monotonicity and there exists a constant independent of $\boldsymbol{y}\in V_n^\omega$ such that $\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})\lesssim\tilde{\mu}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})$. Consequently \eqref{eq:wvn} holds replacing $\tilde{\mu}$ by $\mu.$ For any $L>0$, we sum over all $\Sigma^\omega_n(\alpha)$ such that $\boldsymbol{x}_n^\omega\in\square_L\times(0,h)$ to obtain 
\begin{equation*}
\int_{\bigcup_{n\in N_L}\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \ {\mu}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|v^\omega\big|_{\Sigma_\alpha}|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel \lesssim \int_{\bigcup_{n\in N_L}\Sigma_n^\omega(\alpha)\times(0,h)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \ | \nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}, 
\end{equation*} 
where $N_L\coloneqq \Big\{n \in \mathbb{N} \text{ such that  } \boldsymbol{y}_n^\omega\in\square_L\times(0,h)\Big\}$. Finally we apply Birkhoff Theorem (Theorem \ref{theorem:Birkhoff}) to conclude. \vspace{0.1cm}

Let us consider now $\alpha>h.$ We write for $v\in\mathcal{C}^\infty(\overline{\square_1\times(h,\alpha)})$ \begin{equation*}\forall\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\square_1,\quad v(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \alpha)=v(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, h)+\int^{\alpha}_{h}\partial_{y_d}v(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d} y_d.\end{equation*}By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we get\begin{equation*}\forall\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\square_1,\quad |v(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, \alpha)|^2\leq 2 \left(|v(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, h)|^2+(\alpha-h)\int^{\alpha}_{h}|\partial_{y_d}v(\boldsymbol{y})|^2\mathrm{d} y_d\right).\end{equation*}
We integrate over $\square_1$ to obtain
\begin{multline*}\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)\left|v\big|_{\Sigma_\alpha}\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)d\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\leq 2\left((\alpha-h)\int_{h}^\alpha\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|\partial_{y_d} v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right.\\\left.+\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|\left|v\big|_{\Sigma_h}\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\right).
\end{multline*} By density, this equality also holds for $v\in {\cal W}_0(D)$.  On one hand, by definition \eqref{eq:h} and since $\alpha>h$, we have $\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)\leq 1$ for all $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\square_1$ so we deduce for the first term of the r.h.s.
\begin{equation}\label{eq:1t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{h}^\alpha\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|\partial_{y_d} v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_h^\alpha\int_{\square_1} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].\end{equation}
On the other hand using the fact that $\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)\leq \mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,h)$ for all $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\square_1$ and the first part of the proof, we get for the second term of the r.h.s.
\begin{equation}\label{eq:2t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,\alpha)|\left|v^\omega\big|_{\Sigma_h}\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\int_0^h\int_{\square_1} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{eq:1t} and \eqref{eq:2t} we obtain the desired result.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:wHardy} Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mixing}. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wHardy}\forall v\in\mathcal{W}_0(D),\quad \mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_0^{+\infty} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ \mu(\boldsymbol{y})|v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_0^{+\infty} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ |\nabla v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
We first prove that for all $v\in\mathcal{W}_0(D)$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wHardyL}\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_0^{h} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ \mu(\boldsymbol{y})|v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_0^{h} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ |\nabla v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].\end{equation}
Let us consider the Vorono{\"\i} diagram $(V_n^\omega)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ in $\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times[0,h]$ associated with the point process $\{\boldsymbol{x}_n^\omega\}_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$.
Let $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\omega\in\Omega$. We first prove that the following inequality holds a.s. in $V_n^\omega$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wHardyV}\int_{V_n^\omega}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})|v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\lesssim\int_{V_n^\omega}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}.\end{equation}The proof of \eqref{eq:wHardyV} relies on similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma 1.3 of \cite{beliaev1996darcy}. We reproduce it here for completeness. 
Let $(\rho, \boldsymbol{\theta})$ be the spherical coordinate system centered at $\boldsymbol{x}_n^\omega$. Since $V_n^\omega$ is convex, it can be parametrized as $V_n^\omega\coloneqq \left\{(\rho, \boldsymbol{\theta}), \rho\in(1,R(\boldsymbol{\theta})], \boldsymbol{\theta}\in \mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right\}$. Let $v^\omega\in\mathcal{C}^\infty(\overline{V_n^\omega})$ such that  $v^\omega=0$ on $\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}^\omega_n)$. We have for all $(\rho, \boldsymbol{\theta})\in V_n^\omega$
\begin{equation*}v^\omega(\rho,\boldsymbol{\theta})=\int_1^\rho \partial_r v^\omega(s,\boldsymbol{\theta}) \mathrm{d} s.\end{equation*}
Therefore we derive by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
\begin{equation}\label{eq:polarVorono{\"\i}}|v^\omega(\rho,\boldsymbol{\theta})|^2\leq\int_1^\rho |\partial_r  v^\omega(s, \boldsymbol{\theta})|^2s^{d-1}\mathrm{d} s\int_1^\rho s^{1-d}\mathrm{d} s,\end{equation}
and for $\tilde{\mu}$ a non-negative function on $(1,+\infty)$
\begin{equation*}\int_{1}^{R(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\tilde{\mu}(\rho)|v^\omega(\rho,\boldsymbol{\theta})|^2\rho^{d-1}\mathrm{d}\rho\leq\int_1^{R(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\int_1^\rho |\partial_r  v^\omega(s, \boldsymbol{\theta})|^2s^{d-1}\mathrm{d} s\left(\int_1^\rho s^{1-d}\mathrm{d} s\right)\tilde{\mu}(\rho)\rho^{d-1}\mathrm{d}\rho.\end{equation*}
By Fubini-Tonelli's Theorem we get
\begin{equation*}\int_{1}^{R(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\tilde{\mu}(\rho)|v^\omega(\rho,\boldsymbol{\theta})|^2\rho^{d-1}\mathrm{d}\rho\leq\int_1^{R(\boldsymbol{\theta})} |\partial_r  v^\omega(s, \boldsymbol{\theta})|^2s^{d-1}\left(\int_s^{R(\boldsymbol{\theta})}\left(\int_1^\rho s^{1-d}\mathrm{d} s\right)\tilde{\mu}(\rho)\rho^{d-1}\mathrm{d}\rho \right)\mathrm{d} s.\end{equation*}
For $\tilde{\mu}(\rho)\coloneqq \rho^{-m}$ with $m>d$, $\displaystyle\int_1^{+\infty} \left(\int_1^\rho s^{1-d}ds\right)\tilde{\mu}(\rho)\rho^{d-1}d\rho<+\infty$. We integrate with respect to $\boldsymbol{\theta}$ to get
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{V_n^\omega}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\tilde{\mu}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})|v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\lesssim\int_{V_n^\omega}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}.\end{equation*}
To obtain \eqref{eq:wHardyV} for $v\in{\cal W}_0(D)$, we use a density argument and that for $\boldsymbol{y}\in V_n^\omega$, $\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})\leq\tilde{\mu}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})$. We conclude using Birkhoff Theorem (Theorem \ref{theorem:Birkhoff}).

                                                                                                                   
                  
                                                                                                                                                                                 
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      

Let us now prove \eqref{eq:wHardy}. By integrating by parts, we obtain
\begin{equation*}\begin{array}{ll}\vspace{0.2cm}\displaystyle\int_{h}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})|v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}&\displaystyle\leq \frac{1}{2} \int_{h}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\frac{|v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})}{(y_d+(R^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,h))^{m})^2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y},
  \\&\leq\displaystyle \int_{h}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\frac{\overline{v^\omega}\partial_{y_d}v^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})}{y_d+(R^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,h))^{m}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}+\int_{\square_1}\frac{\left|v^\omega\big|_{\Sigma_h}\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}{h+(R^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,h))^{m}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel.\end{array}
\end{equation*}We deduce the following by applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and using the weighted trace theorem (Lemma \ref{lem:weightedtrace})
\begin{multline*}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{h}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})|v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{h}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\frac{|v^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})|^2}{(y_d+(R^\omega)^m)^2}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{h}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}|\partial_{y_d}v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\+\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_0^{+\infty} \mathbb{1}_{D} \ |\nabla v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].
\end{multline*}
\eqref{eq:wHardy} is then a consequence of Young's inequality.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{rem:wHardyW0D} Note that using similar arguments we can prove that 
\begin{equation}\label{wHardyCC}\int_{D^\omega}\mu^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})|v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\lesssim\int_{D^\omega}|\nabla v^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y},\end{equation}where
for $v^\omega\in W_0(D^\omega)$ for $\omega\in\Omega$, where \begin{equation*}
W_0(D^\omega)\coloneqq \left\{v\in H_{loc}^1\big(D^\omega\big), v=0\,\text{on }\partial \mathcal{P}^\omega, \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_0^{+\infty} \mathbb{1}_{D}\left( \ \mu(\boldsymbol{y})|v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})+|\nabla v|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\right)\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}<+\infty\right\}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:representation_integrale_U_alea} Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mixing}.
A.s.  for $\boldsymbol{y}\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times(L, +\infty)$, $\widehat{U}^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})$ has the following integral representation 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:repint_W10}
\widehat{U}^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}\big) = -2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{z_d}\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{y}, (\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,L)\big) \ \varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big) \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \end{equation}
where $\varphi$ denotes the trace of $\widehat{U}^\omega$ on $\Sigma_L$.
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
Let $\Gamma$ be the Green's function defined in \eqref{fonction_Laplace_Green}. Let us introduce 
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:der_Gamma}
\partial_d\Gamma\coloneqq 
\begin{cases}
  \partial_{z_1}\partial_{z_2}\Gamma  & \text{ for }  d=2 \\
    \partial_{z_1}\partial_{z_2}\partial_{z_3}\Gamma& \text{ for } d=3,
\end{cases}
 \quad\text{and}\quad \pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\coloneqq 
\begin{cases}
  z_1  & \text{ for }  d=2 \\
   z_1z_2& \text{ for } d=3.
\end{cases}
  \end{equation}
  We have for a fixed $R$ and for  $y_d$ large enough that 
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ineq1}
  \int_{\square_R} \left|\partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right| d\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \lesssim \frac{1}{y_d^{3(d-1)}}\quad  \text{and}\quad
  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R}\left| \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\left(y_d^{d-1}+\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\right)\right|d\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel  \lesssim 1 
  \end{equation}
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ineq2}
 \int_{\square_R}  \left|\partial_{z_i}\partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right| d\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \lesssim \frac{1}{y_d^{3(d-1)}} \quad  \text{and}\quad
 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R}  \left|\partial_{z_i}\partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\,\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\right|d\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel  \lesssim \frac{1}{y_d}
  ,\;  i\in\llbracket 1,d\rrbracket,
  \end{equation}
  where the constants depend only on $R$ and $L$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
A straighforward computation yields 
  \[  
  \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z})= \frac{1}{\pi}\frac{z_1z_2}{(z_1^2+z_2^2)^2}  \; \text{ for }  d=2 
  \quad\text{and}\quad  \frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{-15 z_1z_2z_3}{(z_1^2+z_2^2+z_3^2)^{7/2}}\; \text{ for } d=3,
  \]
  it is then easy to show the first inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1}. Moreover, by a change of variable $\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\mapsto \boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel/y_d$, we obtain
  the second inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1}
  \begin{equation*}
  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R} \left|\partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\left(y_d^{d-1}+\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\right)\right|\,d\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \lesssim 
  \left|\begin{array}{l}\vspace{0.2cm}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|u|(1+|u|)\,du}{(u^2+1)^2} \; \text{ for }  d=2 \qquad\qquad
  \\
  \displaystyle \int_{\mathbb{R}^2}\frac{|u_1u_2|(1+|u_1u_2|)\,du_1du_2}{(u_1^2+u_2^2+1)^{7/2}}\; \text{ for }  d=3.\end{array}\right.
  \end{equation*}
  The proof of \eqref{eq:ineq2} can be done using similar arguments.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:limNF} Let $\mathcal{P}$ verify Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mixing}.
$\widehat{U}^\omega$ the unique solution of \eqref{eq:NFtGen}  in $\mathcal{W}_0(D)$ with $F=0$, $G=0$,$\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\in\mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_0)$ and $\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha^N\in \mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_H)$ verifies 

\begin{equation}\label{eq:lim_infty}
 \text{a.s.}\quad \lim_{y_d\to +\infty} \widehat{U}^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, y_d\big) = \mathbb{E}\big[ \varphi\big];\quad \boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel \in \mathbb{R}^{d-1} ,
 \end{equation}
this limit being locally uniform in $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel$ and

 \begin{equation}\label{eq:lim_infty2}
\mathbb{E}\left[\Big| \widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big) - \mathbb{E}\big[ \varphi\big]  \Big|^2 \right] \underset{y_d\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow} 0  \ \quad\text{and}\quad
y_d^{2}  \  \mathbb{E}\left[ \Big|\nabla \widehat{U}^\omega\big(\cdot,y_d\big) \Big|^2 \right] \underset{y_d\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow} 0 \ .  
 \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The proof is based as in section 4.6 of \cite{Basson2006} on the integral representation established in Proposition \ref{prop:representation_integrale_U_alea} and Birkhoff ergodic Theorem.
\\\\
Let us first rewrite $\widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big) - \mathbb{E}[ \varphi]$ using the integral representation \eqref{eq:repint_W10}.  We then have \begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}\label{eq:U-EphiI}
\widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big) -\mathbb{E}[ \varphi]\coloneqq & -2 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \partial_{z_d} \Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \big(\varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)-\mathbb{E}[\varphi]\big)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, \\  
=& -2 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \partial_{z_d} \Gamma\big( \boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L \big)  \partial_{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel}\int_{0}^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \big(\varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w})-\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi\big]\big)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w} \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,\\ 
=& (-1)^d 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \partial_{d}\Gamma \big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \left[\fint_{0}^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}-\mathbb{E}[\varphi]  \right] \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where we used the notations introduced in \eqref{eq:der_Gamma} and also that $\partial_{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel}\coloneqq \partial_{z_1}$ for $d=2$ and $\coloneqq\partial_{z_1}\partial_{z_2}$ for $d=3$ and $\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \coloneqq \int_{0}^{z_1}$ for $d=2$ and $\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{0}^{z_1} \int_{0}^{z_2}$ for $d=3$.


We first prove \eqref{eq:lim_infty}. Let $\varepsilon>0$. By Birkhoff ergodic Theorem (Theorem \ref{theorem:Birkhoff}), we know that there exists $R>0$ such that for all $R'>R$
\begin{equation*}
 \text{a.s.}\quad \left| \fint_{\Box_{R'}} \varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big)\, \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}\ - \ \mathbb{E}[ \varphi] \right| \ \leq \ \varepsilon,\quad \boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in \Box_{R'} \ . 
\end{equation*}
On one hand we obtain then using the second inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1}{
\[
  \left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\Box_R}\hspace{-0.3cm}\partial_{d}\Gamma \big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L \big)\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \left[ \fint_{0}^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel}\varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}-\mathbb{E}[\varphi]\right] \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right| 
   \leq \varepsilon.                                                                                                                                                                                  
\]
\\
On the other hand we have
\begin{multline*}
    \left|\int_{\Box_R} \partial_d\Gamma \big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L \big)\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)  \left[ \fint_{0}^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \varphi^\omega\Big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big)\, \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}-\mathbb{E}[\varphi^\omega]\right] \,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right| \\
       \lesssim  \ \left( \int_{\Box_R} \Big|\varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big)\Big| \, \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}+ R^2\mathbb{E}[ \varphi^\omega]\right) \int_{\Box_R} \left|\partial_d\Gamma \big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \right| \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \ ,  
\end{multline*}
and it suffices to use the first inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1} to obtain the wanted result.}
\\\\
Let us now prove the first limit in \eqref{eq:lim_infty2}.
Let $\varepsilon>0$. By Birkhoff's Theorem, since $\int_{\square_1} \varphi\in L^2(\Omega)$ there exists $R>0$ such that for all $R'>R$
\begin{equation}\label{inegalite_pointb_preuve_prop_alea}
 \mathbb{E}\left[ \left|\fint_{\square_{R'}} \varphi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel - \mathbb{E}\big[ \varphi\big] \right|^2 \right] \ \leq \ \varepsilon \ . 
\end{equation}
By applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and taking the expectation, we obtain for $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$, $y_d>L$, 
\begin{multline}\label{eq:I}
\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big)-\mathbb{E}[ \varphi]\right|^2 \right]
\lesssim \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R} \left| \pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big)\right|\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \\ 
 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R} \left|\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big)  \right| \ \mathbb{E}\left[\left| \fint_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w} -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big] \right|^2\right]\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\\
 +\int_{\square_R} \Big|\partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel ,y_d-L\big) \Big| \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \int_{\square_R} \left| \partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big)  \right| \ \mathbb{E}\left[\left| \int_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} (\varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big])\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}  \right|^2\right] \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel. 
\end{multline}
To bound the second term of the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:I}, note that since $\varphi\in L^2_{loc}\big(\mathbb{R}^{d-1},L^2(\Omega)\big)$, we have
\begin{multline}\label{eq:majoration2}
    \left|\int_{\square_R} \partial_d\Gamma \big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L \big) \mathbb{E}\left[\left| \int_{0}^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} (\varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big])\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}\right|^2\right] \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right| \\
       \lesssim  R^{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_R} \left|\varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big]\right|^2\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}\right]\int_{\square_R} \left|\partial_d\Gamma \big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \right| \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel.  
\end{multline}
We then use the first inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1} to conclude.
{Let us now bound the first term of the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:I}. From \eqref{inegalite_pointb_preuve_prop_alea} we obtain \begin{multline*}
  \left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R}\partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L \big)\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \mathbb{E}\left[\left| \fint_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w} -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big] \right|^2\right]\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right| \\ 
   \leq \varepsilon \ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R} \left|\partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L \big)\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \right| \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel. 
\end{multline*}
By the second inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1} the integral in the right-hand side is bounded. Finally we proved that for $y_d$ large enough
\begin{equation*}
 \mathbb{E}\left[ \left| \widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big)-\mathbb{E}[ \varphi]\right|^2\right] \ \lesssim \ \frac{1}{y_d^{6(d-1)}} + C\varepsilon. 
\end{equation*}
To prove the second limit in \eqref{eq:lim_infty2}, we replace $\widehat{U}^\omega-\mathbb{E}[ \varphi]$ by $\nabla  \widehat{U}^\omega$ and $\partial_d\Gamma$ by $\nabla\partial_d\Gamma$ in \eqref{eq:I} and use the two inequalities of \eqref{eq:ineq2}.}
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:U_comportement_inf_alea_quant}
  If 
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:ergod_quant}
 R^{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\fint_{\square_R} \varphi\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel - \mathbb{E}[\varphi]\right|^2\right]\underset{R\to+\infty}{=}\mathcal{O}(1),
  \end{equation}
  then
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:lim_infty3}
    y_d^{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Big| \widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big) - \mathbb{E}\big[ \varphi\big]  \Big|^2 \right] +    y_d^{d+1} \  \mathbb{E}\left[ \Big|\nabla  \widehat{U}^\omega\big(\cdot,y_d\big) \Big|^2 \right] \underset{y_d\to+\infty}{=}\mathcal{O}(1).  
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Let us fix $\varepsilon>0$. We suppose now that there exist $C\geq0$ and  $R>0$ such that for all $R'>R$
  \begin{equation*}
(R')^{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left| \fint_{\square_{R'}} \varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\ - \ \mathbb{E}[ \varphi] \right|\right] \ \leq C. 
  \end{equation*} 
{
Using similar arguments as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:limNF}, we have for $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$, $y_d>L$, 
\begin{multline*}
\mathbb{E}\left[\left| \widehat{U}^\omega\big( \cdot,y_d\big) - \mathbb{E}\big[ \varphi\big]\right|^2 \right]
\lesssim \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R} \left|\partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big)\right|\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_R} \left|\pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big)  \right| \ \pi(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\mathbb{E}\left[\left| \fint_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w} -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big] \right|^2\right]\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel  
\\+ \int_{\square_R} \Big|\partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel ,y_d-L\big) \Big| \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel  \int_{\square_R} \left| \partial_d\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big)  \right| \ \mathbb{E}\left[\left| \int_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel} \varphi\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\mathbf{w}\big) \  \mathrm{d}\mathbf{w} -\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi \big] \right|^2\right] \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel.
 \end{multline*}
We can bound the second term of the right-hand side as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:limNF} (see \eqref{eq:majoration2}). To bound the first term, we use the second inequality of \eqref{eq:ineq1}.}

{To prove the second part of \eqref{eq:lim_infty3}, we decompose similarly  $\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\nabla  \widehat{U}^\omega\right|^2\right]$ and use the two inequalities of \eqref{eq:ineq2}.}
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
By using \eqref{eq:cascade_champ_loin_alea} and the boundary condition \eqref{eq:condition_limite_champ_loin0_Dirichlet_alea} on $\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}$, we define $u_0^{FF}$ as the unique deterministic solution in $H^1\big(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L}\big)$ of  
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pb_champ_loin_u0_Dirichlet_Alea}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
-\Delta u_0^{FF} - k^2 u_0^{FF} &= f \quad \text{ in } \quad \mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L},\\
u_0^{FF} &= 0 \quad\text{ on }\quad \Sigma_{\varepsilon H},\\
-\partial_{x_d}  u_0^{FF} + \Lambda^k  u_0^{FF} &= 0 \quad \text{ on } \quad \Sigma_{L} .
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
By using \eqref{eq:cascade_champ_loin_alea} and the boundary condition \eqref{eq:condition_limite_champ_loin1_Dirichlet_alea} on $\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}$, we define $u_1^{FF}$ as the unique deterministic solution in $H^1\big(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L}\big)$ of 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pb_champ_loin_u1_Dirichlet_Alea}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
  -\Delta u_1^{FF} - k^2 u_1^{FF} &= 0 \quad \text{ in } \quad \mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L} , \\
  u_1^{FF}&= c_1 \partial_{x_d} u_0^{FF}  \quad \text{ on }\quad \Sigma_{\varepsilon H} ,  \\
-\partial_{x_d}  u_1^{FF} + \Lambda^k  u_1^{FF} &= 0 \quad \text{ on } \quad \Sigma_{L} .
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
where $c_1$ is given by \eqref{eq:constante_c01_Dirichlet_alea}.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
Let $v_\varepsilon$ satisfy 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:modele_eff_Dirichlet_Alea}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
-\Delta v_{\varepsilon} - k^2 v_\varepsilon &= f \quad \text{ in }\,\,\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L} , \\
-\varepsilon c_1 \partial_{x_d} v_\varepsilon  + v_\varepsilon &= 0 \quad \text{ on }\,\, \Sigma_{\varepsilon H} , \\
-\partial_{x_d}  v_\varepsilon + \Lambda^k  v_\varepsilon &= 0 \quad \text{ on } \quad \Sigma_{L} ,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
where $c_1$ is given by \eqref{eq:constante_c01_Dirichlet_alea}.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
There exists $H_0\geq h$ such that for all $H\geq H_0$ \begin{equation}\label{eq:c10pos}c_1(H)>0,\end{equation} where $c_1(H)\coloneqq \mathbb{E}\left[W_1(H)\right]$ and $W_1(H)$ denotes the solution of \eqref{eq:w10} with the normal derivative jump occurring at $\Sigma_H$.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Let H'>H. One can easily establish thanks to proposition \ref{prop:Utnwellposed} that
\begin{equation*}W_1(H')=W_1(H)+(y_d-H)\chi_{(H,H')}(y_d)+(H'-H)\chi_{(H',+\infty)}(y_d).\end{equation*}Taking the limit as $y_d\to+\infty$, one gets
\begin{equation*}c_1(H')=c_1(H)+(H'-H).\end{equation*}
Choosing $H'$ large enough leads to \eqref{eq:c10pos}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:effmod}
For $H$ large enough and $L>\varepsilon H$, the following estimate holds
\begin{equation}\label{eq:effmod}
\left\| u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon u_1^{FF}-v_\varepsilon\right\|_{H^2(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L})}\underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2).
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Let $e_\varepsilon\coloneqq u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon u_1^{FF}-v_\varepsilon$. Applying the Fourier Transform in the $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel$-direction to \eqref{eq:pb_champ_loin_u0_Dirichlet_Alea}, \eqref{eq:pb_champ_loin_u1_Dirichlet_Alea} and 
\eqref{eq:modele_eff_Dirichlet_Alea}, one obtains that 
\[ 
\widehat{e_\varepsilon}(\zeta,x_d)=\varepsilon^2\frac{c_1\partial_{x_d}\widehat{u}^{FF}_1(\zeta,\varepsilon H)}{1-\mathrm{i}\varepsilon c_1\sqrt{k^2-|\boldsymbol{\zeta}|^2}}e^{\mathrm{i} \sqrt{k^2-|\boldsymbol{\zeta}|^2}x_d}
\]
where $\widehat{e_\varepsilon}$ (resp. $\partial_{x_d}\widehat{u}^{FF}_1$) denotes the Fourier Transform of $e_\varepsilon$ (resp. $\partial_{x_d}{u_1}$) and by convention $\sqrt{k^2-|\boldsymbol{\zeta}|^2}=\mathrm{i}\sqrt{|\boldsymbol{\zeta}|^2-k^2}$. Using the characterization of Sobolev spaces from the Fourier Transform (see \eqref{eq:def_Hs}), one easily shows that 
\[ 
\|e_\varepsilon\|_{H^2(\mathcal{B}_{\varepsilon H,L})}\lesssim \varepsilon^2 \|\partial_{x_d}{u}_1^{FF}\|_{H^{3/2}(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})}.
\]
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- hypothesis-2: body_md -->
\makeatletter\def(Mix){($L^{\infty}$)}\makeatother\label{hyp:Linfty}
The distance to the nearest particule $R$ defined in \eqref{eq:def_R} satisfies $$\sup_{y_d\in(0,h)}R(\cdot,y_d)\in L^{\infty}(\Omega, L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})).$$
<!-- end hypothesis-2 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rem:WP_Linfty}
  Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty} corresponds to a stronger assumption than \nameref{hyp:mixing} for the particle's distribution. A direct consequence is, since $\mu$ is bounded from below, that the near-fields solutions of problem of the form \eqref{eq:NFtGen} are in
\begin{multline*}
 \mathcal{H}_0(D)\coloneqq  \left\{\text{a.s. } V^\omega\in H^1_{loc}(D^\omega), V^\omega\big(\cdot,y_d\big) \text{ stationary for any }y_d>0, \right. \\\left. \text{a.s.  } V^\omega=0 \text{ on } \mathcal{P}^\omega,\,\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_0^{+\infty}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\Big( \frac{|V^\omega|^2}{1+y_d^2}+|\nabla V^\omega|^2\Big) \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right] <+\infty \right\}.
\end{multline*}
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:err1}
Let $u\in H^1(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})$ and $U\in\mathcal{H}_0(D)$ be such that 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:firstrate}
  \mathbb{E}[|U|^2(\cdot,y_d)]+y_d^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[|\nabla U|^2(\cdot,y_d)]\underset{y_d\to+\infty}{=}o(1).
\end{equation}
Then $\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_\varepsilon:\boldsymbol{x}\mapsto u(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\,U(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon})$ satisfies 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:energy_estimate}
\left\|\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}))}\leq \eta(\varepsilon)\|u\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\quad\text{and}\quad
+\left\|\mathcal{U}_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^2(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L}))}\leq \eta(\varepsilon)\|u\|_{H^1(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})},
\end{equation}
where $\eta(\varepsilon)$ tends to $0$ as $\varepsilon$ tends to $0$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
By stationarity of $U$ (and a change of variable in the first integral), we get immediately
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inter1}
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_0^L\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|u(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)|^2\left|U\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^2\mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\mathrm{d} x_d\right]
=\varepsilon\,\int_0^{L/\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|u(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)|^2\mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel.
\end{equation}
Let $\delta>0$. By \eqref{eq:firstrate}, there exists $L_0\geq 0$ such that for all $y_d\geq L_0$, 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inter2}
  \mathbb{E}[|U|^2(\cdot,y_d)]+y_d^2\mathbb{E}[|\nabla U|^2(\cdot,y_d)]\leq \frac{\delta}{2L}.
\end{equation}
We can then write
\begin{equation*}
\int_0^{{L}/{\varepsilon}} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d\leq\int_0^{L_0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d+\frac{\delta}{2\varepsilon}.
\end{equation*}
This and the previous relation prove that for $\varepsilon$ small enough, $\left\|\mathcal{U}_\varepsilon\right\|^2_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}))}\leq \delta\,\|u\|^2_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}$.
Moreover
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inter3bis}
  \|\nabla\mathcal{U}_\varepsilon\|_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}_{L',L}))}\leq\|\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel} u\,U\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\|_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}_{L',L}))}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|u\,\nabla U\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\|_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}_{L',L}))}.
\end{equation} 
The first term can be dealt with in the same way as $\left\|u(\cdot)U\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|^2_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}))}$ replacing $u$ by $\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}u$.
For the second term, we use \eqref{eq:inter2} to obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inter3}
  \begin{array}{ll}
  \displaystyle \frac{1}{\varepsilon^2}\|u(\cdot)\nabla U\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\|^2_{L^2(\Omega, L^2(\mathcal{B}_{L',L}))}
  &=\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|u\|^2_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\int_{\frac{L'}{\varepsilon}}^{\frac{L}{\varepsilon}} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\nabla U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d
  \\&\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{2L\varepsilon}\|u\|^2_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}\int_{\frac{L'}{\varepsilon}}^{\frac{L}{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{y_d^{2}}\mathrm{d} y_d\lesssim \delta\|u\|^2_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}.
\end{array}\end{equation}
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:betterNFest}
Let $u\in L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})$. Consider $U\in\mathcal{H}_0(D)$ such that  
\begin{equation}\label{eq:convu}
  y_d^{d-1}\mathbb{E}[|U|^2(\cdot,y_d)]+y_d^{d+1}\mathbb{E}[|\nabla U|^2(\cdot,y_d)]\underset{y_d\to+\infty}{=}\mathcal{O}(1).\end{equation} 
  Then $\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_\varepsilon:\boldsymbol{x}\mapsto u(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\,U(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon})$ satisfies 
  \eqref{eq:energy_estimate} with 
\begin{equation*}
\eta(\varepsilon)\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}{ll}\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{1/2}|\log \varepsilon|^{1/2}\right)&\text{for }\; d=2,
  \\\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{1/2})&\text{for }\; d=3.\end{array}\right.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
We follow the same ideas as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:err1}. Thanks to \eqref{eq:convu} we can improve our initial estimate of $\displaystyle\int_0^{\frac{L}{\varepsilon}} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d$ as such
\begin{align*}
\int_0^{\frac{L}{\varepsilon}} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d&\leq\int_0^{L'}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d+C'\int_{L'}^{\frac{L}{\varepsilon}}y_d^{-(d-1)}\mathrm{d} y_d\\&=\int_0^{L'}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\left(\cdot,y_d\right)\right|^2\right]\mathrm{d} y_d+ C'\left\{\begin{array}{ll}\displaystyle\log \frac{L}{\varepsilon}-\log L'&\textrm{for}\, d=2,\\\displaystyle(L'^{-1}-\frac{\varepsilon}{L})&\textrm{for}\, d=3.\end{array}\right.
\end{align*}
This inequality combined with \eqref{eq:inter1} gives us the first result. Moreover, one has to adapt \eqref{eq:inter3} using that
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{\frac{L'}{\varepsilon}}^{\frac{L}{\varepsilon}}y_d^{-(d+1)}=\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^d).
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
Note that we cannot prove that Problem \eqref{eq:NF} with $n=2$ is well posed since 
  it is not clear in general that ${\bf G}_1$, which depends on $\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel} U_1^{NF}$ (see \eqref{eq:NF}), is in $\mathcal{L}^2(D)$. The function $\widetilde{U}_2^{\omega, NF}$ is somehow the part of ${U}_2^{\omega, NF}$ that is well-defined and enables us to perform the error analysis.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:error} 
  Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty}.
For all $L>L'>\varepsilon H$, the following estimates hold
\begin{equation}\label{eq:err1a}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-w_\varepsilon\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)} \underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=} o(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:err1b}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-(u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon u_1^{FF})\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)} \underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=} o(\varepsilon).
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:error1} 
For all $L>L'>\varepsilon H$, under Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty}, the following estimates hold
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cor1a}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-\widetilde{w}_\varepsilon\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)} \underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=} o(\varepsilon),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cor1b}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-v_\varepsilon\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)} \underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=} o(\varepsilon).
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Let us write the problem verified by $w^\omega_\varepsilon$ in $\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}$.
$w^\omega_\varepsilon$ satisfies on one hand for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{B}^\omega_L$
\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{rl}
\vspace{0.2cm}\displaystyle-(\Delta+k^2)w^\omega_\varepsilon (\boldsymbol{x})
=&\displaystyle f(\boldsymbol{x})-\varepsilon(\Delta +k^2 )\Big[(U_{1}^{\omega,NF}+ \varepsilon\widehat{U}_2^{\omega,NF})\left( \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon}\right) \Big] \\ \vspace{0.2cm}
=&\displaystyle f(\boldsymbol{x})+ \left[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon} \Delta_{\boldsymbol{y}}- \nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel} \cdot\nabla_{\boldsymbol{y}} - \varepsilon \Big( \Delta_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}+k^2\Big)\right](U_{1}^{\omega,NF}+ \varepsilon\widetilde{U}_2^{\omega,NF})\left( \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon}\right) \\
=&\displaystyle f(\boldsymbol{x}) - \varepsilon \nabla\cdot\left[\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}( U_{1}^{\omega,NF}+ \varepsilon\widetilde{U}_2^{\omega,NF})\left( \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]-\varepsilon k^2( U_{1}^{\omega,NF}+ \varepsilon\widetilde{U}_2^{\omega,NF})\left( \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon}\right),
\end{array}
\end{equation*} 
where we used that $U_1^{NF}$ and $\widetilde{U}_2^{NF}$ are solutions of near-field problems with no volume source terms and that $\varepsilon\nabla [V(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\boldsymbol{x}/\varepsilon)]=\varepsilon[\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}V](\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\boldsymbol{x}/\varepsilon)+[\nabla_y V](\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\boldsymbol{x}/\varepsilon)$. On the other hand $w^\omega_\varepsilon$ verifies the following jumps and boundary conditions
\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{|l}
\displaystyle \Big[ w^\omega_\varepsilon\Big]_{\varepsilon H}=0,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\Big[ \partial_{x_d}w^\omega_\varepsilon\Big]_{\varepsilon H}=0,\\ 
\displaystyle -\partial_{x_d}w^\omega_\varepsilon+ik\gamma w^\omega_\varepsilon=\varepsilon^2\ ik\gamma \widetilde{U}_2^{\omega,NF}|_{\Sigma_0}\quad\text{on\,}\; \Sigma_0,\quad\text{and}\quad \displaystyle w^\omega_\varepsilon=0\;\text{on\,}\; \partial \mathcal{P}^\omega,\\
 \partial_{x_d} w^\omega_\varepsilon + \Lambda^k w^\omega_\varepsilon=\displaystyle [\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{y_d} + \Lambda^k](\varepsilon U_{1}^{\omega,NF}+ \varepsilon^2\widetilde{U}_2^{\omega,NF})\quad\text{on\,} \Sigma_L,
 \end{array}
\end{equation*}where $\Lambda^K$ denotes the DtN operator introduced in the definition \ref{definition_DtN_sol_ref_alea}. 


We can now write the variational formulation verified by $e_\varepsilon\coloneqq  u_\varepsilon-w_\varepsilon$ in $L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)$. 
For all $v \in L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)$, 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:FVerror}
   \mathbb{E}\left[a_\varepsilon^\omega(e_\varepsilon^\omega,v^\omega)\right] =\varepsilon \ell_\varepsilon^1(v)+\varepsilon^2\ell_\varepsilon^2(v)+\varepsilon\ell_\varepsilon^3(v),
\end{equation}
where $a_\varepsilon^\omega$ is defined in \eqref{eq:a_epsomega} and 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:FVerror_ell}
  \begin{array}{ll}
\ell_\varepsilon^1(v)&= -\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel} \left(U_{1}^{\omega,NF} +\varepsilon \widetilde{U}_{2}^{\omega,NF}\right)\big(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot \overline{ \nabla v^\omega}(\boldsymbol{x})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\right.\\
&\vspace{0.2cm}\displaystyle\hspace{2cm}+ k^2\int_{\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \left(U_{1}^{\omega,NF} +\varepsilon \widetilde{U}_{2}^{\omega,NF}\right)\big(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\varepsilon} \big) \overline{v^\omega}(\boldsymbol{x})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\left.\right]\\
\ell_\varepsilon^2(v)&= \displaystyle ik\gamma\,\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Sigma_0} \widetilde{U}_{2}^{\omega,NF}\Big|_{\Sigma_0}\big(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}{\varepsilon}\big) \overline{v^\omega}\big|_{\Sigma_0}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right]\\
\ell_\varepsilon^3(v)&= \displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{L}}\overline{v^\omega}|_{\Sigma_L}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel) \left[\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\partial_{y_d} + \Lambda^k \right] \left(U_{1}^{\omega,NF} +\varepsilon \widetilde{U}_{2}^{\omega,NF}\right)\Big|_{\Sigma_L}\big(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel;\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}{\varepsilon}\big)  \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel \right].
\end{array}
\end{equation}
Let us now bound the norm of each anti-linear form appearing in \eqref{eq:FVerror}. For $\ell_\varepsilon^1$, both volume integrals 
can be bounded applying Lemma \ref{lem:err1} to $U_1^{NF}, \nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel} U_1^{NF}, \widetilde{U}_2^{NF}$ 
or $\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}\widetilde{U}_2^{NF}$ with $U$ being $V_i, i=1,2$ and $u$ being $\partial_{x_d} u_i^{FF}\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}$ or $\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}\partial_{x_d} u_i^{FF}\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}$. Note that $V_i, i=1,2$ verify \eqref{eq:firstrate} by Proposition \ref{prop:limNF} and $\partial_{x_d} u_i^{FF}\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}, \nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}\partial_{x_d} u_i^{FF}\in H^{1}(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}), i=1,2$ ($\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}$ being identified to $\mathbb{R}^{d-1}$). By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get 
\begin{equation*}
|\ell_\varepsilon^1(v)|\lesssim \eta(\varepsilon) \|v\|_{L^2(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}))},
\end{equation*}
where $\eta(\varepsilon)$ tends to $0$ as $\varepsilon$ tends to $0$.

For $\ell_\varepsilon^2$, we use that under Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty} the following trace theorem (analogue to 
Lemma \ref{lem:weightedtrace}) holds for all $U\in\mathcal{H}_0(D)$
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\left|U\big|_{\Sigma_0}(\cdot)\right|^2\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\left|\nabla U\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].
\end{equation*}
We obtain for $u\in L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})$ and  $U\in\mathcal{H}_0(D)$
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Sigma_0}\left|u(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)U\big|_{\Sigma_0}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|^2\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right]
  \lesssim\|u\big|_{\Sigma_0}\|^2_{L^2(\Sigma_0)}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\square_1}\left|\nabla U\right|^2(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].
\end{equation*}
Applying this result to $\widetilde{U}_2^{NF}$ we get by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
\begin{equation*}
|\ell_\varepsilon^2(v)|\lesssim \|v\big|_{\Sigma_0}\|_{L^2(\Omega,L^2(\Sigma_0))}.
\end{equation*}
We conclude with a trace inequality in $L^2(\Omega, H^1(\mathcal{B}^\omega_L))$ 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tracewoutmu}
  \forall \alpha\in[0,L],\quad \|\mathbb{1}_D v|_{\Sigma_\alpha}\|_{L^2(\Omega,L^2(\Sigma_\alpha))}\lesssim \|\mathbb{1}_Dv\|_{L^2(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_L^\omega))}.
\end{equation}
                                                                           
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
Finally let us focus on $\ell_\varepsilon^3$.
Since by Proposition \ref{prop:limNF} $\|V_i(\cdot,y_d)\|_{L^2(\Omega)}+y_d\|\nabla V_i(\cdot,y_d)\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\underset{y_d\to+\infty}{=}o(1)$  for $i=1$ and $2$, we deduce that
\begin{equation*}
\|V_i\big|_{\Sigma_{{L}/{\varepsilon}}}\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}o(1),\quad\text{and}\quad \|\partial_{y_d}V_i|_{\Sigma_{{L}/{\varepsilon}}}\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}o(\varepsilon).
\end{equation*}
By Definition \eqref{eq:U1v} and \eqref{eq:U2t} of $U_1^{NF}$ and $\widetilde{U}_2^{NF}$ and thanks to the continuity of $\Lambda^k$ and \eqref{eq:tracewoutmu} we deduce that 
\begin{equation*}
|\ell_\varepsilon^3(v)|\lesssim \eta(\varepsilon)\,\|v\|_{L^2(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_L^\omega))},
\end{equation*}
Therefore, we have shown that, for any $ v\in L^2\Big( \Omega,H^1\big(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L}\big)\Big)$
\begin{equation}
\big| \varepsilon \ell_\varepsilon^1(v)+\varepsilon^2\ell_\varepsilon^2(v)+\varepsilon\ell_\varepsilon^3(v)\big| \leq  \varepsilon \,\eta(\varepsilon)\, \| v\|_{L^2(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_L^\omega))}.
\end{equation}
Thanks to the well-posedness result of Corollary \ref{cor:wpue} we deduce that
\begin{equation*}
\| e_\varepsilon\|_{L^2(\Omega,H^1_0(\mathcal{B}_L^\omega))}\underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=} \varepsilon \,\eta(\varepsilon). 
\end{equation*} 
This ends the proof of \eqref{eq:err1a}.

To prove \eqref{eq:err1b} we decompose the error in two parts
\begin{equation*}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-(u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon u_1^{FF})\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)}\leq \left\| u_\varepsilon-w_\varepsilon\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)}+\left\| \varepsilon U_1^{NF}+\varepsilon^2 \widetilde{U}_2^{NF}\right\|_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)}
\end{equation*}
From \eqref{eq:err1a}, we know that the first term of the right-hand side is a $o(\varepsilon)$. For the second term we use the result of Lemma \ref{lem:err1}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
To prove \eqref{eq:cor1a} we need to estimate the norm of $w_\varepsilon-\widetilde{w}_\varepsilon$ in $V_L\coloneqq L^2(\Omega, H_1(\mathcal{B}_L^\omega))$. To do so we decompose the error in three parts
  \begin{multline}\label{eq:decomperr}
\left\| w_\varepsilon-\widetilde{w}_\varepsilon\right\|_{V_L}\leq \left\| u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon u_1^{FF}-v_\varepsilon\right\|_{V_L}
+\varepsilon \left\| \partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon  u_1^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}V_1\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{V_L}\\ +\varepsilon^2\left\|  \partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}V_2\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{V_L}.
\end{multline}
Proposition \ref{prop:effmod} allows us to estimate the first term of the right-hand-side. 
Let us now deal with the two remaining terms. Since $V_i, i=1,2$ verify \eqref{eq:firstrate} by Proposition \ref{prop:limNF} 
and $\partial_{x_d} u_i^{FF}\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}},\partial_{x_d} v_\varepsilon^{FF}\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\in H^{s}(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})$ for any $s$, we get deriving inequalities similar to \eqref{eq:inter3bis} and \eqref{eq:inter3}, and from Lemma \ref{lem:err1}
\begin{multline}\label{eq:V1e}
\left\| \partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon  u_1^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}V_1\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{V_L}\lesssim \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon  u_1^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\|_{L^2(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})}\\
+\eta(\varepsilon)\|\partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon  u_1^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\|_{H^1(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})},
\end{multline} 
and  
\begin{multline}\label{eq:V2e}
\left\| \partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}V_2\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{V_L}\lesssim \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|\partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\|_{L^2(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})}+\eta(\varepsilon)\|\partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\|_{H^1(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})}
\end{multline}
where $\eta(\varepsilon)$ tends to $0$ as $\varepsilon$ tends to $0$. From \eqref{eq:effmod} we know moreover that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:difftrace}
\|\partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon  u_1^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\|_{H^1(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})}\lesssim \varepsilon^2, \quad\text{and}\quad\|\partial_{x_d} (u_0^{FF}- v_\varepsilon)\big|_{\Sigma_{\varepsilon H}}\|_{H^1(\Sigma_{\varepsilon H})}\lesssim \varepsilon.
\end{equation} 
Inserting \eqref{eq:difftrace} into \eqref{eq:V1e} and \eqref{eq:V2e} allows us to conclude.
Finally, to prove \eqref{eq:cor1b} we combine \eqref{eq:err1b} and \eqref{eq:effmod}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:fluct} Let $L\geq \varepsilon H$. Providing that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and the mixing hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mix}, $W_1^{\omega}|_{\Sigma_L}$ verifies for all $f\in L^\infty(\Sigma_L)$ with compact support\begin{equation}\label{eq:fluct}\textrm{Var}\left[\int_{\Sigma_L}f(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)W_1^\omega|_{\Sigma_L}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\right]\lesssim\|f\|^2_{L^2(\Sigma_L)}.\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:errorimproved} Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies  \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and \nameref{hyp:mix} . For all $R>0$ and $L>L'>\varepsilon H$,  the following estimates hold
\begin{equation}\label{eq:err1improved}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-w_\varepsilon\right\|^2_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)}+\left\| u_\varepsilon-(u_0^{FF}+\varepsilon u_1^{FF})\right\|^2_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)} \underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}\left\{\begin{array}{ll}\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|\right)&\text{for } d=2,\\\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^3)&\text{for } d=3,\end{array}\right.
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:error2improved}Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies  \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and \nameref{hyp:mix}. For all $R>0$ and $L>L'>\varepsilon H$, the following estimates hold
\begin{equation*}
\left\| u_\varepsilon-\widetilde{w}_\varepsilon\right\|^2_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}^{\omega}_{L})\big)}+\left\| u_\varepsilon-v_\varepsilon\right\|^2_{L^2\big(\Omega,H^1(\mathcal{B}_{L',L})\big)} \underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}\left\{\begin{array}{ll}\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|\right)&\text{for } d=2,\\\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^3)&\text{for } d=3,\end{array}\right.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{thm:fluct}, under Hypotheses \nameref{hyp:mix} and \nameref{hyp:Linfty}, for all $R\geq1$
 \begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}[W_1^{\omega}|_{\Sigma_L}]-\fint_{\square_R}W_1^{\omega}|_{\Sigma_L}\right|^2\right]\lesssim R^{-(d-1)}.
\end{equation*} 
Consequently by Proposition \ref{prop:U_comportement_inf_alea_quant} we know that $V_1$, defined in \eqref{eq:U1v}, verifies 
\begin{equation*}
  y_d^{d-1}\mathbb{E}[|V_1|^2(\cdot,y_d)]+y_d^{d+1}\mathbb{E}[|\nabla V_1|^2(\cdot,y_d)]\underset{y_d\to+\infty}{=}\mathcal{O}(1).
\end{equation*} 
We then obtain for $L>\varepsilon H$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ingr1}
\|V_1|_{\Sigma_{{L}/{\varepsilon}}}\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\right),\quad\text{and}\quad \|\partial_{y_d}V_1|_{\Sigma_{{L}/{\varepsilon}}}\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\underset{\varepsilon\to0}{=}\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\right).
\end{equation}
Inserting \eqref{eq:ingr1} and the result of Corollary \ref{cor:betterNFest} in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:error} yields \eqref{eq:err1improved}.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The proof is identical as the proof of Corollary \ref{cor:error1} but we take advantage here of the improved estimate of Corollary  \ref{cor:betterNFest}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- hypothesis-3: body_md -->
\makeatletter\def(Mix){(Mix)}\makeatother\label{hyp:mix}
There exists a non-increasing weight function \mbox{ $\pi:\mathbb{R}^+\to\mathbb{R}^+$} with super-algebraic decay such that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies for all $\sigma(\mathcal{P})$- measurable random variable $F(\mathcal{P})$,
\begin{equation} \label{eq:decorr}
\textrm{Var}\left[F(\mathcal{P})\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_1^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \left(\partial^{osc}_{\mathcal{P}, \mathcal{L}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)} F(\mathcal{P})\right)^2 \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel \ell^{-(d-1)} \pi(\ell-1) d\ell\right],
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\coloneqq \displaystyle\square_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\times(0,h)$ is the portion of the layer with width 
$\ell\geq 0$ centered at $\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel, {h}/{2}\right)$ and the oscillation $\partial^{osc}_{\mathcal{P}, \mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)} F(\mathcal{P})$
 of $F(\mathcal{P})$ with respect to $\mathcal{P}$ on $\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$ is defined by: 
 \begin{multline}\label{eq:der_osc}
  \partial^{osc}_{\mathcal{P}, \mathcal{L}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}F(\mathcal{P})\coloneqq \textrm{sup ess}\left\{F(\mathcal{P}'), \mathcal{P}'\cap\left(\mathbb{R}^d\setminus \overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right)=\mathcal{P}\cap\left(\mathbb{R}^d\setminus \overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right)\right\}\\-\textrm{inf ess}\left\{F(\mathcal{P}'), \mathcal{P}'\cap\left(\mathbb{R}^d\setminus \overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right)=\mathcal{P}\cap\left(\mathbb{R}^d\setminus \overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right)\right\}.
\end{multline}
<!-- end hypothesis-3 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:aux} 
  Let $f\in L^2(\Sigma_L)$ and $\boldsymbol{g}\in L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d$ with compact support. There exists a unique process $u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}$ such that a.s. $u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega\in W_0(D^\omega)$ is a weak solution of \eqref{eq:uf} and 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:energufg}
  \|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}\|_{L^2(D^\omega)}\lesssim\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{ L^2(\Sigma_L)}+\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{L^2(D^\omega)^d}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
It suffices to use Lax-Milgram Theorem.
The variational formulation reads for
\begin{equation}\label{eq:WFug}
  \forall v^\omega\in W_0(D^\omega),\quad \int_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega\cdot\nabla\overline{v}^\omega=\int_{D^\omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla\overline{v}^\omega+\int_{\Sigma_L}f\overline{v^\omega}.
\end{equation}
The coercivity of the sesquilinear form is ensured by \eqref{eq:wPoincareCC}.
Since $f\in L^2(\Sigma_L)$ has compact support, $\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\in L^2(\Sigma_L)$ and
\begin{equation*}
  \left|\int_{\Sigma_L}f\overline{v}^\omega\right|\leq \|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_L)}\| v^\omega\|_{W_0(D^\omega)}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:aux} 
  Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies  \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and \nameref{hyp:mix}. There exists $C_0\simeq 1$ such that for all $f\in L^\infty(\Sigma_L)$ and $\boldsymbol{g}\in L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d$ with compact support, the solution $u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}$ of the auxiliary problem \eqref{eq:uf} statisfies 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:momentuf}
\|\mathbb{1}_{D}\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+, L^q(\Omega))}\lesssim\|f\|_{ L^p(\Sigma_L)}+\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{ L^p(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d},
\end{equation}
for all $|p-2|,|q-2|\leq \frac{1}{C_0}$.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:momentsnw1} 
Assume that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies hypotheses  \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and \nameref{hyp:mix}. 
For all $q\geq 2$, $R_1\geq1$, the following estimate holds for all $L$ large enough
\begin{equation}\label{eq:momentnw1}
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^L\mathbb{1}_{D}|\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim 1,
\end{equation}
where the constant depends on $R_1$ and $L$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop:fluctw1}
Providing that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies hypotheses \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and \nameref{hyp:mix}, $\nabla W_1^{\omega}$ verifies for $\boldsymbol{g}\in L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d$ with compact support, $q\gg 2$ and $R_1\geq1$
 \begin{equation}\label{eq:fluctw1}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla W_1^\omega\mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{y}\right|^q\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
  \lesssim\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^L\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- proposition-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:localnorm} 
  Suppose that $\mathcal{P}$ verifies Hypotheses \nameref{hyp:Linfty} and \nameref{hyp:mix}. Consider ${\text{\large $\chi$}}\in C_c^\infty(\square_1\times(0,1))$ 
  such that $\int_{\square_1\times (0,1)}\text{\large $\chi$}=1$ and define for $r>0$, $\text{\large $\chi$}_r(\boldsymbol{x})\coloneqq r^{-d}\text{\large $\chi$}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}\right)$. 
For all $R\gg 1$, $L\gg 1$, $\alpha\in(0,1)$ and $q\geq 2$  
\begin{equation*}
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_R}\int_0^L\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
\lesssim 1+\left(\int_0^{2L}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_0^{+\infty}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\text{\large $\chi$}_{R^\alpha}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, x_d-y_d)\nabla W_1^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right|^q\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\mathrm{d} x_d\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},
\end{equation*}
where the constant only depends on $\text{\large $\chi$}$.
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
We prove here \eqref{eq:momentnw1} for $q\gg 2$. By Jensen's inequality the result can then be extended to all $q\geq 2$. 
By stationarity of $W_1$ we have on one hand for all $R_1, R_2\geq 1$, $L\geq H$
\begin{align}\label{eq:buck1}
  &\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^L \mathbb{1}_D|\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
                                                                                                                                                
  =\mathbb{E}\left[\fint_{\square_{R_2}}\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^L \mathbb{1}_D |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)} \int_0^L \mathbb{1}_D|\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
  \nonumber \\&\qquad\qquad\leq\left(\frac{R_1+R_2}{R_1}\right)^{\frac{(d-1)(q-1)}{2q}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1+R_2}}\int_0^L  \mathbb{1}_D|\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}
    \fint_{\square_{R_2}}\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^L  \mathbb{1}_D |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
   \nonumber \\&\qquad\qquad\leq\left(\frac{R_1+R_2}{R_1}\right)^{\frac{(d-1)(q-1)}{2q}}\frac{1}{R_2^{\frac{d-1}q}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1+R_2}}\int_0^L  \mathbb{1}_D|\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q-1}{2}}
    \left(\int_{\square_{R_2}}\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^L  \mathbb{1}_D |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
   \nonumber \\&\qquad\qquad\leq\left(\frac{R_1+R_2}{R_1}\right)^{\frac{(d-1)}{2}}\frac{1}{R_2^{\frac{d-1}q}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1+R_2}} \int_0^L \mathbb{1}_D|\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}},
  \end{align}
  where we used Jensen's inequality since $x\mapsto \sqrt{x}$ is concave.
  On the other hand according first to Proposition \ref{prop:localnorm} for $R\gg 1$, $L\gg1$ and $q\gg2$ and second to Proposition \ref{prop:fluctw1} 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:buck2}
\begin{aligned}
    \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_0^{L} |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
    &\lesssim1+\left(\int_0^{2L}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_0^{+\infty}\text{\large $\chi$}_{{R}^\alpha}(\boldsymbol{y},x_d-y_d)\nabla W_1(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right|^q\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\mathrm{d} x_d\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
    \\&\lesssim 1+\left(\int_0^{2L}\|\text{\large $\chi$}_{{R}^\alpha}(x_d-\cdot)\|^2_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)}\mathrm{d} x_d\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^L |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
    \\&\lesssim1+R^{-\frac{\alpha d}{2}}R_1^{\frac{d-1}2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^L |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
                                                                                                                                     
  \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Injecting \eqref{eq:buck2} with $R=R_1+R_2$ into the final inequality of \eqref{eq:buck1} yields
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}} \int_0^L\mathbb{1}_D |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\left(\frac{R_1+R_2}{R_1}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\frac{1}{R_2^{\frac{d-1}{q}}}
  +\frac{(R_1+R_2)^{\frac{(1-\alpha)d-1}{2}}}{R_2^{\frac{d-1}{q}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^L |\nabla W_1|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
\end{equation*}
With $\alpha$ chosen so that $(1-\alpha)d<1$ and $R_2$ large enough, the second term of the right-hand side can be absorbed into the left hand-side and we get the result.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proposition-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:osc}
  Let $\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$ and $\ell\geq 1$. 
  For $f\in L^\infty(\Sigma_L)$ with compact support
\begin{equation*}
