  \left|\partial^{osc}_{\mathcal{P}, \mathcal{L}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_{\Sigma_L}f(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)W_1^{\omega}|_{\Sigma_L}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\right|
  \lesssim(\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))^2\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega \|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))},
\end{equation*} 
where  $u_f^\omega\coloneqq u_{f,\boldsymbol{0}}^\omega$ and $\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$ is the smallest distance such that $\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\setminus\overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}$ contains at least one particle (see Figure \ref{fig:do}), 
\textit{i.e.}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dp}
\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\coloneqq \text{argmin}\left\{d>\ell, \quad\exists j\in\mathbb{N}, B(\boldsymbol{x}_j^\omega)\subset\mathcal{L}_{d}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\setminus\overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right\}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-12 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Let $\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$ and $\ell\geq 1$.
Consider $\mathcal{P}^\omega$ a realization of the particle distribution in the layer and let $\mathcal{P}'$ be a repartition of particles satisfying the hard-core assumption and such that
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{P}'\cap\left(\mathbb{R}^d\setminus \overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right)=\mathcal{P}^\omega\cap\left(\mathbb{R}^d\setminus \overline{\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\right).\end{equation*}
We let $W_1'$ denote the solution of problem \eqref{eq:w10} where $\mathcal{P}^\omega$ (resp. $D^\omega$) is replaced by $\mathcal{P}'$ (resp. $D'\coloneqq \mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+\setminus\overline{\mathcal{P}'}$). 

                           
                                                                                                                                                                                         
                                                                                                  
                                                                                                                                                    
               

\noindent\textit{Step 1:} We first prove that $W_1^{'}$ is well-defined and verifies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:claim1}
  \|\mathbb{1}_{D'}\nabla W_1^{'}\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\lesssim\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\,\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.\end{equation}
Consider the set of particles of $\mathcal{P}^\omega$ (resp. $\mathcal{P}'$) intersecting with $\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$, \textit{i.e.}
 \begin{gather*}
  \mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega),\,\, \text{with }\quad\mathcal{I}_\ell^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\coloneqq \left\{i\in\mathbb{N}, \quad \boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega\in\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\right\},
  \\\left(\text{resp. }\,\,\mathcal{P}_\ell'\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}'(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}B(\boldsymbol{x}_i'),\,\, \text{with }\quad\mathcal{I}'_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\coloneqq \left\{i\in\mathbb{N}, \quad \boldsymbol{x}'_i\in\mathcal{L}_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\right\}\right).
\end{gather*} 
Let $\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\text{Hull}\left(\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega\right)$ where $\text{Hull}$ denotes the convex hull. Let $\text{\large $\chi$}$ be a smooth cut-off function such that $\text{supp} \text{\large $\chi$} \subset \mathcal{S}$
                    
                                                                                                                                             
                                                                                                                                    
then $X^\omega\coloneqq W_1'-\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega} W_1^\omega(1-\chi)$ verifies the following problem in $D'$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:x}
\left\{
\begin{array}{l}
-\Delta X^\omega= \Delta (\chi W_1^\omega)  \;\text{ in } \quad D'\setminus\Sigma_H,  \\
  -\partial_{y_d} X^\omega = 0 \; \text{ on } \quad \Sigma_0, \quad\text{and}\quad 
 X^\omega = 0\; \text{ on } \quad \mathcal{P}', \\
    \Big[X^\omega\Big]_H  = 0 \quad  \text{ and }\quad \Big[ -\partial_{y_d}X^\omega\Big]_H = 0.
\end{array}
\right.
\end{equation}
Since $\nabla (\chi W_1)\in [L^2(D')]^d$ with compact support, there exists a unique $X^\omega\in W_0(D')$ by proposition \ref{prop:aux}. Hence $W_1'$ is well-defined. Let us now show that it verifies estimate \eqref{eq:claim1}.

Let $\widetilde{D}^\omega\coloneqq D^\omega\cup\overline{\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}.$
$Z^\omega\coloneqq \mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell'}W_1'- \mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}W_1^\omega$  verifies
                              
         
                   
                                                                                                                                                   
                                                                 
                                                                                         
                                                                                                                                                                          
                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                          
             
         
                
                                                                  
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weakz}
  \forall v\in W_0(\widetilde{D}^\omega),\quad \int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega} \nabla Z^\omega\cdot\nabla\overline{ v}=\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n} \,\overline{v}+\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell'}\nabla W_1^{'}\cdot\boldsymbol{n} \,\overline{v}.
\end{equation} 
To simplify notations we use here integrals on the boundary of $\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega$ and $\mathcal{P}_\ell'$ to denote the duality product $H^{-{1}/{2}}, H^{{1}/{2}}$. 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  

The coercivity of the associated sesquilinear form is a direct consequence of the weighted Poincar\'e inequality \eqref{eq:wPoincareCC}. 
The continuity of the linear form is ensured by the trace theorem and a Poincare's inequality that holds with a constant proportional to $\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$ since at least one particle lies in $\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$. We obtain for all $i\in\mathcal{I}'_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel), j\in\mathcal{I}_\ell^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$, $v\in W_0(\widetilde{D}^\omega)$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tracelayer}
  \|v\|_{H^{{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_j^\omega))}+\|v\|_{H^{{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i'))}\lesssim \ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\,\|\nabla v\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.
\end{equation} 

Let us now estimate $\|\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}$ for $i\in\mathcal{I}_\ell^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$. 
We proceed by duality. Let $\psi_i\in H^{{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))$ and $\Psi_i\in H^1(\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))$ 
a lifting of $\psi$ such that $\textrm{supp} \Psi_i\subset \displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$, $\Psi_i|_{\partial  B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega)}=\psi_i\delta_{i,j}$ for all $j\in\mathcal{I}_\ell^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$ and 
\begin{equation*}
  \|\Psi_i\|_{H^1(\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\leq\|\psi_i\|_{H^{{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}.
\end{equation*} 
We write the weak formulation associated to \eqref{eq:w10} in $\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\nabla \overline{\Psi_i}=\int_{\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega)}\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\, \overline{\psi_i}.
\end{equation*}
By definition of the $H^{-1/2}$-norm we deduce that
\begin{equation*}
  \|\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}\leq\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.
\end{equation*}
We can prove similarly for $i\in\mathcal{I}'_\ell(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$, $\|\nabla W_1^{'}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i'))}\leq\|\mathbb{1}_{D'}\nabla W_1'\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.$

Testing \eqref{eq:weakz} with $v=Z^\omega$, we get
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega} |\nabla Z^\omega|^2=-\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\, \mathbb{1}_{D'}\overline{W_1^{'}}
  -\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell'}\nabla W_1^{'}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\,  \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\overline{W_1^\omega},
\end{equation*} 
and therefore
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega}|\nabla Z^\omega|^2\lesssim \ell^{\omega}_{+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\| \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\| \mathbb{1}_{D'}\nabla W_1^{'}\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.
\end{equation*}
Claim \eqref{eq:claim1} follows from Young's inequality.\vspace{0.2cm}


\noindent\textit{Step 2: Sensitivity of $W_1|_{\Sigma_L}$} \vspace{0.2cm}

Recall that $u_f\coloneqq u_{f,\boldsymbol{0}}$ denotes the unique weak solution in $W_0(D^\omega)$ of \eqref{eq:uf} with $\boldsymbol{g}=\boldsymbol{0}$. The weak formulation associated to \eqref{eq:uf} reads for all $v\in W_0(\widetilde{D}^\omega)$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weakug}
  \int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega\cdot\nabla\overline{ v}=\int_{\Sigma_L}f\overline{v}+\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\, \overline{v}.
\end{equation} 
We evaluate \eqref{eq:weakz} for $v=\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\overline{u_{f}^\omega}$ and \eqref{eq:weakug} for $v=\overline{Z^\omega}.$ After subtracting both expressions and since $u_f^\omega=W^\omega_1=0$ on $\partial\mathcal{P}^\omega$, we obtain 
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{\Sigma_L}f Z^\omega=-\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n} \,\mathbb{1}_{D'}W_1^{'}-\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell'}\nabla W_1^{'}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\, \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}u_{f}^\omega.
\end{equation*}
Next we can estimate $\|\nabla u_{f}^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}$ for $i\in\mathcal{I}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$ using similar steps as in the estimate of $\|\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_j^\omega))}$. We deduce that

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                                                               
                                                                                     
                          
\begin{equation*}
  \|\nabla u_{f}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-{1}/{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}\leq\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.
\end{equation*}
Therefore
\begin{equation*}
  \left|\int_{\Sigma_L}f (\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell'}W_1'- \mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}W_1^\omega)\right|\lesssim \ell^{\omega}_{+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\,\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega \|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\,\|\mathbb{1}_{D'}\nabla W_1^{'}\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.
\end{equation*}
Using \eqref{eq:claim1} we deduce that
\begin{equation*}
  \left|\int_{\Sigma_L}f (\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell'}W_1'- \mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{P}_\ell^{''}}W_1^{''})\right|\lesssim \ell^{\omega}_{+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\,\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{f}^\omega \|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\,\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^{\omega}\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^{\omega}_{+}}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.
\end{equation*}
where $\mathcal{P}_\ell''$ satisfies the same assumption than $\mathcal{P}_\ell'$ and $W_1''$ is the associated solution. By definition of \eqref{eq:der_osc}, this allows us to conclude.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- proposition-13: body_md -->
\label{prop:pbdemiespa_bienpose}
For all $\varphi$ such that $\varphi\in \mathcal{W}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_L)$, there exists a unique $V\in \mathcal{W}(\mathcal{B}^\infty_L)$ that is a.s. solution of \eqref{eq:halfspace}.
<!-- end proposition-13 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{Lemme_decompo_VT_alea}
The function $V_T$ given by \eqref{eq:VT_intrep} is in $\mathcal{H}^1(\mathcal{B}_L^\infty)$ and satisfies a.s.
\begin{equation}\label{eq:halfspaceT}
    \left\{ 
    \begin{aligned}
    - \Delta V_T^\omega + T^{-1}V_T^\omega &= 0 \quad \quad \text{ in  } \quad \mathcal{B}^\infty_L \ , \\
    V_T^\omega &= \varphi^\omega \quad \text{ on } \quad \Sigma_L \ . 
    \end{aligned}
    \right.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{rem:comportement_infini_reg}
Even if it is not fundamental for the sequel, note that the function $V_T$ given by \eqref{eq:VT_intrep} is even the unique solution of \eqref{eq:halfspaceT} in  $\mathcal{H}^1(\mathcal{B}_L^\infty)$

Let us also mention that if $\varphi^\omega\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_L)$ then it is easy to show using Lax Milgram's Theorem that $V^\omega\in H^1(B_L)$. The difficulty in our setting comes from the fact that $\varphi\in\mathcal{W}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_L)$ but as we shall see the stationarity plays a fundamental role.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let ${V}_T$ be given by the integral expression of \eqref{eq:VT_intrep}. It is easy to show (using similar arguments than in \cite{colton2013integral} or in \cite[Theorem 3.2]{chandler1997impedance} for instance) that a.s. for any $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$, $y_d>L$, $\widetilde{V}_T^\omega$ is in $C^2(\mathcal{B}_L^\infty)$, it satisfies the first equation of (\ref{eq:halfspaceT}) and the boundary condition of (\ref{eq:halfspaceT}) in the trace sense. This implies in particular that a.s. $V_T^\omega\in H_{loc}^1(\mathcal{B}^\infty_L)$ .
By stationarity of $\varphi$, we have for $ \boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel$, $\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel \in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$ and $ y_d>L$, 
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
\widetilde{V}_T^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel,y_d \big) 
                                                                                                                                                                                    
&= -2 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{y_d} G_T^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \ \varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel+\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel) \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, \\ 
&= -2 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{y_d} G_T^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \ \varphi^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}\omega}(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel= \widetilde{V}_T^{\tau_{\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel}\omega}\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,y_d \big). 
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
which shows that $\big(\omega,\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\big)\mapsto V^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,y_d\big)\text{is stationary for any } y_d>L$ .

Moreover,  we have on one hand for $L'>L$,

 \begin{equation*}
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \vspace{0.2cm}\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_{L'}^{+\infty} \big|{V}_T^\omega \big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,y_d\big)\big|^2 \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right]&\displaystyle= \ 4 \  \mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_1} \int_{L'}^{+\infty} \left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L) \  \varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big) \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right|^2  \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right], \\ 
\vspace{0.2cm}&\displaystyle \lesssim    \ \int_{\square_1}\int_{L'}^{+\infty} \mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}(\mu^\omega)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,L) \left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right|  \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right] \\\vspace{0.2cm}&\displaystyle\mathbb{E} \left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right|  \big|(\mu^\omega_{|_{\Sigma_L}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big)\big|^2 \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right] \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y},  \\ 
\vspace{0.2cm}&\displaystyle \lesssim   \ \int_{L'}^{+\infty} \left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right|  \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right)^2dy_d\, \mathbb{E}\Big[ h_{|_{\Sigma_L}}|\varphi|^2\Big] \\&\displaystyle\lesssim \ \big\| \mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_L)},
 \end{array}
 \end{equation*}
where we used that $\mu(\cdot,L)$ and $\varphi$ are stationary, $\mathbb{E}[\mu^{-1}(\cdot,L)]\lesssim L^2$ and that (thanks to \eqref{eq:fonction_Laplace_Green_T} and \eqref{eq:GreenT2d}) for $L'$ large enough
\[
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right|  \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel
 \lesssim \frac{1}{y_d-L}\quad \text{for}\;y_d>L'.\]
On the other hand, for $\chi\in \mathcal{C}_c^\infty\big(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\big)$ such that $0\leq \chi\leq 1$ and $\chi=1$ on $\left(-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right)^{d-1}$, we can write
\begin{multline*}
  \mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{L'} \big|{V}_T^\omega \big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,y_d\big)\big|^2 \, \mathrm{d}y_d\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\right] \lesssim \mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{L'} \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}  \partial_{y_d} G_T \big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel -\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, y_d-L\big) \ \chi  \varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right|^2\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} \right] \\   + \mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_L^{L'}  \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{y_d} G_T\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \ (1-\chi)  \varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big)\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right|^2 \,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right].
\end{multline*}
Since a.s.  $\chi \varphi^\omega \in {H}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_L)$ it is easy to show (see Remark \ref{rem:comportement_infini_reg}) that a.s.
\[
   \int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{L'} \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}  \partial_{y_d} G_T \big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel -\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, y_d-L\big) \ \chi \ \varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right|^2\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} <+\infty
\]
Moreover, using that when $\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\in\square_1$, $(1-\chi)(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel)= 0$ when $\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\in\square_1$, we deduce that
\begin{align*}
 &\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1} \int_L^{L'} \left| \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \partial_{y_d}G_T\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \ (1-\chi)\varphi^\omega(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel) \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2 \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} \right],\\
&\quad \qquad\qquad\lesssim \int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{L'} \mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_1} (\mu^\omega)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,L)\left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right|  \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right]  \\ &\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad \mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_1} \left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right| \  \big|(\mu^\omega_{|_{\Sigma_L}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big)\big|^2 \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right] \,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}, \\ 
&\quad \qquad\qquad\lesssim   \ \int_{L}^{L'} \left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_1} \left|\partial_{y_d}G_T(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L)\right|  \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel \right)^2\,\mathrm{d}y_d\,\mathbb{E}\Big[ h_{|_{\Sigma_L}}|\varphi|^2\Big], \\&\quad \qquad\qquad\displaystyle\lesssim \ \big\| \mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi\big\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}^2(\Sigma_L)}.\end{align*}
Note that $\partial_{y_d}G_T\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_1\times(L,L'))$ is a regular function with an exponential decay at infinity (see \eqref{eq:fonction_Laplace_Green_T}-\eqref{eq:GreenT2d}).

Similar results can be derived for $\nabla V_T^\omega$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
Problem \eqref{eq:halfspaceD} is well posed in $\mathcal{W}_0(\mathcal{B}^\infty_L)$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
The variational formulation associated with \eqref{eq:halfspaceD} is given by 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pbDir_FV}
\forall W\in \mathcal{W}_0(\mathcal{B}^\infty_L), \quad \mathbb{E} \left[\int_{\square_1} \int_{L}^{+\infty} \nabla \widetilde{V} \cdot \nabla\overline{ W}\, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}  \right] \ = \ -T^{-1}\mathbb{E} \left[\int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{+\infty}  V_T \overline{W}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} \right]. 
\end{equation}The coercivity of the sesquilinear form can be directly deducted from Hardy's inequality \eqref{inegalite_Hardy_alea}.
From the integral representation of $V_T$, we deduce that $y_d\mapsto V_T(\cdot,y_d)$ is exponentially decaying and in particular
\begin{equation*}
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_1}\int_L^{+\infty} \big( 1 + y_d^2\big) | V_T|^2 \, \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} \right] \ < \ +\infty \ . 
\end{equation*}
We have then by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
\begin{equation*}
 T^{-1} \left|\mathbb{E}\left[ \int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{+\infty}   V_T \overline{W} \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} \right] \right|\lesssim T^{-1}\|\sqrt{1+y_d^2}V_T\|_{\mathcal{L}^2(\mathcal{B}^\infty_L)}\| W\|_{\mathcal{W}^{0}}  \ . 
\end{equation*}
The linear form in the r.h.s of \eqref{eq:pbDir_FV} is then continuous in $\mathcal{W}^{0}$. We conclude by Lax-Milgram's Theorem.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proposition-14: body_md -->
\label{prop:regir} Let $Z:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times(L,+\infty)$ be defined as 
\begin{equation*}
 Z(\boldsymbol{y})=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\partial_{y_d}\Gamma\big(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,y_d-L\big) \ \varphi^\omega\big(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\big) \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel,\end{equation*}Then $Z\in \mathcal{W}(\mathcal{B}^\infty_L)$ and $Z^\omega$ is a.s. solution of \eqref{eq:halfspace}.
<!-- end proposition-14 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
We follow the same steps as in the proof of Lemma \ref{Lemme_decompo_VT_alea} to show that $Z$
 satisfies \eqref{eq:halfspace}, that a.s. it belongs to $H^1_\text{loc}( \mathcal{B}_L^\infty)$ and that $Z(\cdot,y_d)$ is stationary for $y_d>L$. To prove that 
 \begin{equation*}
    \mathbb{E}\Big[ \int_{\square_1}\int_{L}^{+\infty} |\nabla \widehat{U}^\omega |^2\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y} \Big] <+\infty 
    \end{equation*} 
  the calculations are similar to the proof of Lemma \ref{Lemme_decompo_VT_alea}, provided one uses that
  \[
  \partial_{y_d}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{z}} \Gamma(\boldsymbol{z})=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\left((z_d)^2+|\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel|^2\right)^{d/2}}\right)\quad\text{as}\; |\boldsymbol{z}|\rightarrow+\infty
  \]
  and 
  \[
  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel}{\Big(\big(y_d-L\big)^2+|\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel|^2\Big)^{d/2} } \, \lesssim \frac{1}{y_d-L}\quad\text{for}\;y_d>L'\quad\text{and}\quad \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\setminus\square_1} \frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel}{\Big(\big(y_d-L\big)^2+|\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel|^2\Big)^{d/2} } <+\infty.
  \]
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:Gehring} Let $1<q<s$ and $Q_0$ be a reference cube in $\mathbb{R}^d$. Take $F\in L^s(2Q_0)$ and $G\in L^q(2Q_0)$ two non-negative functions. Suppose that for every cube $Q\subset Q_0$
 \begin{equation*}\left(\fint_Q G^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq b\fint_{2Q} G+b\left(\fint_{2Q} F^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\theta \left(\fint_{2Q}G^q\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},\end{equation*}where $b>1$ and $\theta\geq 0$. There exists $\theta_0=\theta_0(q,s,d)$ and $\eta_0=\eta_0(b,d,q,s)>0$ such that if $\theta\leq\theta_0$, then for all $q\leq p\leq q+\eta_0$
  \begin{equation*}\left(\fint_{Q_0} G^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\left(\fint_{2Q_0} F^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+ \fint_{2Q_0}G,\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:DCZ}Let $1\leq p_0<p_1$, $C\in[\frac{1}{2}, 2]$ and $Q_0$ be a reference cube in $\mathbb{R}^d$. Take $F\in L^{p_1}(2Q_0)$ and $G\in L^{p_1}(2Q_0)$. Suppose that for every cube $Q\subset Q_0$, there exists two measurable functions $F_{Q,0}$ and $F_{Q,1}$ such that $|F|\leq |F_{Q,0}|+|F_{Q,1}|$ and $|F_{Q,1}|\leq |F|+|F_{Q,0}|$ on $2Q_0$
  \begin{align*}
  \left(\fint_{Q} |F_{Q,0}|^{p_0}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_0}}&\leq C_1 \left(\fint_{CQ} |G|^{p_0}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_0}},\\
  \left(\fint_{\frac{1}{C}Q} |F_{Q,1}|^{p_1}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_1}}&\leq C_2 \left(\fint_{Q} |F_{Q,1}|^{p_0}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_0}},
  \end{align*}with $C_1,C_2>0$.
 Then for all $p_0<p<p_1$\begin{equation*}
  \left(\fint_{Q_0} |F|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim \fint_{2Q_0} |F|+\left(\fint_{2Q_0} |G|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
\noindent\textit{Step 1.} We prove that a.s. for all $2\leq p\leq 2+\frac{1}{C_0}$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:upperpf}
  \left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^+} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
  \lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^+} |\boldsymbol{g}|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mu^{-\frac{p}{2}} |f|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.\end{equation}

Let $R>0$. Take $\chi$ a smooth cut-off function such that $\chi|_{\square_R\times(0,R)}\equiv1$, $\chi\geq 0$, $\textrm{supp}\chi\subset\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)$ and $\vspace{0.1cm}\left|\nabla \chi\right|\lesssim \displaystyle\frac{1}{R}$. We test \eqref{eq:WFug} with $v\coloneqq \chi(u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}-c)$ where $c$ is a constant. We obtain 
\begin{multline*}
  \int_{\square_R\times(0,R)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\leq\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\,\boldsymbol{g}\cdot(\chi\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega+(u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega-c)\nabla \chi)
  \\+\mathbb{1}_{R\geq L}\int_{\square_{2R}\cap\Sigma_L}f\chi(u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega-c)-\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\,\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega\cdot(u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega-c)\nabla \chi.
\end{multline*}
Using Young's inequality with $K\geq 1$ we get
\begin{multline*}
  \int_{\square_R\times(0,R)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\lesssim\frac{1}{R^2}\left(\frac{K^2}{2}+\frac{1}{2K^2}\right)\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega-c|^2+\frac{1}{K^2}\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2
  \\+\frac{1}{2K^2}\int_{\square_{2R}\cap\Sigma_L}\mu|u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega-c|^2+\frac{K^2}{2}\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)}|\boldsymbol{g}|^2+\frac{K^2}{2}\int_{\square_{2R}\cap\Sigma_L}\mu^{-1}|f|^2.
\end{multline*}
Choosing $c\coloneqq \displaystyle\fint_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega$, we apply Poincar\'e-Sobolev inequality to the first term and the trace Theorem to the third term of the right-hand side
\begin{multline*}
  \left(\int_{\square_{R}\times(0,R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
  \lesssim K\left(\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{p^\star}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^\star}}+\frac{1}{K}\left(\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
  \\+K\left(\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} \left(|\boldsymbol{g}|^2+ (2R\mu)^{-1}|f|^2\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},
\end{multline*}
with $p^\star={2d}/{(d+2)}$ and where we have identified the function $\mu^{-1}|f|^2\in L^1(\square_{2R}\cap\Sigma_L)$ to a function in $L^1(\square_{2R}\times(0,2R))$
that is independent of $x_d$. We conclude by applying Gehring's Lemma (Lemma \ref{lem:Gehring})
with $q={2}/{p^\star}$, $F\coloneqq \left(|\boldsymbol{g}|^2+(2R\mu)^{-1}|f|^2\right)^{{p^\star}/{2}}$ 
and $G\coloneqq |\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{p^\star}$ that for all $2\leq p\leq 2+ {1}/{C_0}$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:upperp}
    \left(\fint_{\square_{R}\times(0,R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim 
    \left(\fint_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{p^\star}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^\star}}+\left(\fint_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} |\boldsymbol{g}|^p+\fint_{\square_{2R}} (2R\mu)^{-\frac{p}{2}}|f|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},
  \end{equation}
where $C_0\coloneqq \eta_0p^\star$.
By Jensen's inequality and since $2>p^*$, \eqref{eq:upperp} yields
\begin{multline*}
  \left(\int_{\square_{R}\times(0,R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim R^{(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2})d}\left(\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
  \\+\left(\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} |\boldsymbol{g}|^p+\int_{\square_{2R}}  \mu^{-\frac{p}{2}}|f|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.
\end{multline*}
\eqref{eq:energufg} implies 
\begin{multline*}
  \left(\int_{\square_{R}\times(0,R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim R^{(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2})d}\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+} |\boldsymbol{g}|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mu^{-1} |f|^2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
  \\+\left(\int_{\square_{2R}\times(0,2R)} |\boldsymbol{g}|^p+\int_{\square_{2R}} \mu^{-\frac{p}{2}}|f|^p\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.
\end{multline*}
Next we let $R\to+\infty$ and obtain \eqref{eq:upperpf} for $2\leq p\leq 2+ \frac{1}{C_0}$. 
          
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  
                                 
                                           
                                                                                               
                                                                                                                                                                                               


\noindent\textit{Step 2: Proof of \eqref{eq:momentuf} for $2\leq q<p\leq 2+\frac{1}{C_0}$} Let $2\leq p_0\leq p_1\leq 2+\frac{1}{C_0}$ and $R>0$. Let $u^{\omega, 0}_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}$ be the unique solution in $W_0(D^\omega)$ of 
\begin{equation*}
\left\{
\begin{array}{l}
-\Delta u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0} = \nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{g}\mathbb{1}_{\square_R\times(0,R)})\; \text{in} \;D^\omega\setminus(\Sigma_H\cup\Sigma_L),  \\
  -\partial_{y_d} u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0}  = 0 \;\text{on} \,\,\Sigma_0, \quad\text{and}\quad 
  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0} =0\;\text{on}\,\,\partial\mathcal{P}^\omega,\\
 \Big[u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0} \Big]_H  = 0 \quad \text{and}\quad\Big[ -\partial_{y_d} u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0} \Big]_H=0, \\ 
 \Big[u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0}\Big]_L  = 0 \quad \text{and}\quad\Big[ -\partial_{y_d} u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0} \Big]_L=f\mathbb{1}_{\square_R}. 
\end{array}
\right.
\end{equation*}
We apply \eqref{eq:upperpf} to $u^{\omega, 0}_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}$ 
\begin{align*}
\int_{\square_R}\int_{(0,R)}\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0}|^{p_0}\right]&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^+} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,0}|^{p_0}\right]\\&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_R}\int_{(0,R)} |\boldsymbol{g}|^{p_0}+ \int_{\square_R} \mu^{-\frac{p_0}{2}}|f|^{p_0}\right]\\&\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_{(0,2R)} |\boldsymbol{g}|^{p_0}+\int_{\square_{2R}} \mu^{-\frac{p_0}{2}}|f|^{p_0}\right].\end{align*}
On the other hand $u^{\omega, 1}_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}\coloneqq u^{\omega}_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}-u^{\omega, 0}_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}$ verifies in $D^\omega_R\coloneqq \square_R\times(0,R)\setminus\overline{\mathcal{P}^\omega}$
\begin{equation*}
\left\{
\begin{array}{l}
-\Delta u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1} =0\; \text{in}\;D^\omega_{R,R}\setminus(\Sigma_H\cup\Sigma_L),  \\
  -\partial_{y_d} u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1} = 0 \;\text{on} \,\,\Sigma_0\cap\square_R,\quad\text{and}\quad 
  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}=0\;\text{on} \,\,\partial\mathcal{P}^\omega,\\
 \Big[u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1} \Big]_{\Sigma_H\cap\square_R}  = 0 \quad \text{and}\quad\Big[ -\partial_{y_d} u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1} \Big]_{\Sigma_H\cap\square_R}=0,\\ 
 \Big[u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}\Big]_{\Sigma_L\cap\square_R}  = 0 \quad \text{and}\quad \Big[ -\partial_{y_d} u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1} \Big]_{\Sigma_L\cap\square_R}=0.
\end{array}
\right.
\end{equation*}
We first deduce from the triangle inequality in $L^{\frac{p_1}{p_0}}(\square_{\frac{R}{2}}\times(0,\frac{R}{2}))$ the following
\begin{equation*}
\left(\fint_{\square_{\frac{R}{2}}}\fint_{(0,\frac{R}{2})}\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}|^{p_0}\right]^{\frac{p_1}{p_0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_1}}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{\frac{R}{2}}}\fint_{(0,\frac{R}{2})} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}|^{p_1}\right)^{\frac{p_0}{p_1}}\right]^{\frac{1}{p_0}}.\end{equation*}
From \eqref{eq:upperp} with $g=0$ on $\square_R\times(0,R)$ and $f=0$ on $\Sigma_L\cap\square_R$, we obtain
\begin{equation*}\left(\fint_{\square_{\frac{R}{2}}}\fint_{(0,\frac{R}{2})}\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}|^{p_0}\right]^{\frac{p_1}{p_0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_1}}\lesssim \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R}}\fint_{(0,R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}|^{p^\star}\right)^{\frac{p_0}{p^\star}}\right]^{\frac{1}{p_0}}.\end{equation*}
Finally with Jensen's inequality (since $p_0\geq p^\star$) we conclude that
\begin{equation*}\left(\fint_{\square_{\frac{R}{2}}}\fint_{(0,\frac{R}{2})}\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}|^{p_0}\right]^{\frac{p_1}{p_0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_1}}\lesssim \mathbb{E}\left[\fint_{\square_{R}}\fint_{(0,R)} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^{\omega,1}|^{p_0}\right]^{\frac{1}{p_0}}.\end{equation*}We are now in position to apply Lemma \ref{lem:DCZ} with $C=2$,
\begin{align*} F\coloneqq \mathbb{E}[|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}|^{p_0}|]^{\frac{1}{p_0}}, &\quad G\coloneqq \mathbb{E}[|\boldsymbol{g}|^{p_0}+\mu^{-\frac{p_0}{2}}|f|^{p_0}]^{\frac{1}{p_0}},\\
F^0\coloneqq  \mathbb{E}[|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^0|^{p_0}|]^{\frac{1}{p_0}}, &\quad F^1\coloneqq  \mathbb{E}[|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}^1|^{p_0}|]^{\frac{1}{p_0}}.\end{align*}We obtain for all $p_0<p<p_1$, $R_0>0$
\begin{multline*}\left(\fint_{\square_{R_0}}\fint_{(0,R_0)}\mathbb{E}[|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}|^{p_0}|]^{\frac{p}{p_0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\fint_{\square_{R_0}}\fint_{(0,R_0)}\mathbb{E}[|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}|^{p_0}|\\+\left(\fint_{\square_{2R_0}}\fint_{(0,2R_0)}\mathbb{E}[|\boldsymbol{g}|^{p_0}]^{\frac{p}{p_0}}+\fint_{\square_{2R_0}}\mu^{-\frac{p_0}{2}}|f|^{p_0}]^{\frac{p}{p_0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.\end{multline*}We let $R_0\to+\infty$ and obtain \eqref{eq:momentuf} for $2\leq p\leq 2+ \frac{1}{C_0}$. 

\noindent\textit{Step 3: Proof of \eqref{eq:momentuf} for $2-{1}/{2C_0}\leq p<q\leq 2$.}
We proceed by duality:
\begin{equation*}\|\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+,L^{q}(\Omega))}=\sup_{\boldsymbol{h}\in L^{p'}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+,L^{q'}(\Omega))}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+} h\cdot\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}\right]| \|h\|_{L^{p'}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+,L^{q'}(\Omega))}=1\right\}.\end{equation*}Moreover testing the variational formulation verified by $u_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}$ with $v\coloneqq \overline{u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}}$ yields\begin{equation}\label{eq:vfuh}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times \mathbb{R}^+}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \nabla u_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}\cdot \nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+} \boldsymbol{h}\cdot\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}.\end{equation}Testing the variational formulation verified by $u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}$ with $v\coloneqq \overline{u_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}}$ and subtracting \eqref{eq:vfuh} gives
\begin{equation*}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+} \boldsymbol{h}\cdot\nabla u_{f,\boldsymbol{g}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla u_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} fu_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}.\end{equation*}Moreover \begin{multline*}\sup\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+} \boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla u_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}fu_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}\right]| \|h\|_{L^{p'}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+,L^{q'}(\Omega))}=1\right\}\leq\left(\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^{d-1},L^q(\Omega))}\right.\\\left.+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L^p(\mathbb{R}^{d-1},L^q(\Omega))}\right)\sup\left\{\|\nabla u_{0,\boldsymbol{h}}\|_{ L^{p'}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+,L^{q'}(\Omega))}| \|h\|_{L^{p'}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+,L^{q'}(\Omega))}=1\right\}.\end{multline*}
Since $2\leq q'<p'\leq 2+\frac{1}{C_0}$ we conclude thanks to step 2.2. By interpolation estimate \eqref{eq:momentuf} holds then for all $|p-2|, |q-2|<\frac{1}{C_0'}$ with $C_0'=8C_0$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:highmix} 
    If $\mathcal{P}$ satisfies Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mix}, then for all $q<+\infty$
and  all $\sigma(\mathcal{P})$- measurable random variable $F(\mathcal{P})$
\begin{equation*} 
\mathbb{E}\left[|F(\mathcal{P})-\mathbb{E}[F(\mathcal{P})]|^q\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim q^2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_1^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \left(\partial^{osc}_{\mathcal{P}, \mathcal{L}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)} F(\mathcal{P})\right)^2 \mathrm{d} \boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}} \ell^{-\frac{(d-1)q}{2}} \pi(\ell-1) d\ell\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
\noindent\textit{Step 1: Sensitivity of the $\nabla W_1$} \vspace{0.2cm} Let $\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in \mathbb{R}^{d-1}$ and $\ell\geq 1$. In what follows we use the same notations as in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:osc}. Let us first  prove that \begin{equation}\label{eq:oscg}\left|\int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla Z^\omega\right|\lesssim(\ell^\omega_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))^2\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega \|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.\end{equation}

Let  $\boldsymbol{g}\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times \mathbb{R}^+, \mathbb{R}^d).$ We introduce the auxiliary problem $u_{\boldsymbol{g}}$ solution of \eqref{eq:uf} with $f\coloneqq 0.$
The weak formulation associated to \eqref{eq:uf} reads for all $\varphi\in W_0(\widetilde{D}^\omega)$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weakugbis}
  \int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}\cdot\overline{\nabla \varphi}=\int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega}  \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\overline{\nabla \varphi}+\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\, \overline{\varphi}.\end{equation}We evaluate \eqref{eq:weakz} for $\varphi=\overline{u_{\boldsymbol{g}}}$ and \eqref{eq:weakugbis} for $\varphi=\overline{Z}.$ After substracting both expressions we obtain 
\begin{equation*}\int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega} \boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla Z^\omega=-\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega\cdot\boldsymbol{n} \,\mathbb{1}_{D'}W_1^{'}-\int_{\partial\mathcal{P}_\ell'}\nabla W_1^{'}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\, \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega.\end{equation*} 
Using the same arguments as in the proof of proposition \ref{prop:osc}, we can estimate by duality $\|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}$ 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
                                                                                                                                                                                                                              
                                                                                           
                           
and obtain,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dugn}\|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B(\boldsymbol{x}_i^\omega))}\leq\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{eq:tracelayer} and \eqref{eq:dugn} we conclude that
\begin{equation*}\left|\int_{\widetilde{D}^\omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla Z^\omega\right|\lesssim2(\ell^\omega_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega \|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\|\mathbb{1}_{D'}\nabla W_1^{'}\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}.\end{equation*}
We get \eqref{eq:oscg} using \eqref{eq:claim1}.\vspace{0.2cm}

\noindent\textit{Step 2:}
 Let $g\in\mathcal{C}_c^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times \mathbb{R}^+)$ and $2\leq q<+\infty$.
The second step consists in applying the higher-order version (Lemma \ref{lem:highmix}) of the mixing hypothesis (Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:mix}) to $\displaystyle\int_0^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla W_1(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}$.
\begin{align*}
  &\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_0^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla W_1(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right|^q\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}
  \\&\leq q^2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_1^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} \left(\partial^{osc}_{\mathcal{P}, \mathcal{L}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)} \int_0^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla W_1(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right)^2 \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}} \ell^{-\frac{q(d-1)}{2}} \pi(\ell-1) \mathrm{d}\ell\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}
  \\&\lesssim q^2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_1^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}} (\ell^\omega_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))^4\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega \|^2_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla W_1^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathcal{L}_{\ell^\omega_+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel))}^2 \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}} \right.
    \left.\ell^{-\frac{q(d-1)}{2}} \pi(\ell-1) \mathrm{d}\ell\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}.
  \end{align*}
Next we use Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty} to bound a.s. and for a.e. $\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\in R^{d-1}$, $\ell^{\omega}_+(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)$ by $\ell+R^+$. 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:f1}
  \begin{aligned}\begin{multlined}
    \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_0^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega} \boldsymbol{g}\cdot\nabla W_1(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right|^q\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\lesssim q^2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_1^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\right.\right.
    \\\left.\left.\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}(\ell+R^+)^{(d+1)q}\ell^{-\frac{q(d-1)}{2}} \pi(\ell-1) \mathrm{d}\ell\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}
  \end{multlined}\\
  \begin{multlined}\hfill\lesssim q^2\left(\int_1^{+\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\right.\right.\\\left.\left.\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right]^{\frac{q}{2}}(\ell+R^+)^{(d+1)q}\ell^{-\frac{q(d-1)}{2}} \pi(\ell-1) \mathrm{d}\ell\right)^{\frac{2}{q}}
  \end{multlined}\end{aligned}\end{equation}
  By duality we can write
\begin{multline*}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}
  \\=\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel X\right],
\end{multline*}
where $\tilde{q}=\frac{q}{q-2}$ and the supremum is computed over positive random variables $X$ independent of $\boldsymbol{x}$. We artificially introduce a spatial average over $\square_{R_1}$ for a given $R_1\geq 1$ by noticing that by Fubini-Tonelli's Theorem for $h\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:aver}
  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}h(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}h(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel.
\end{equation} 
We get
\begin{align*}
  &\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel X\right]
  \\&=\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel X\right]
  \\&\begin{multlined}
    \leq\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left(1+\frac{R_1}{\ell+R^+}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^++R_1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\right.
    \\\left.\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel X\right]
  \end{multlined}\end{align*}
  By H\" older's inequality and the stationarity of $\nabla W_1$ we obtain then for $q\gg2$ such that $|\tilde{q}-1|\leq \frac{1}{2C_0}$ 
\begin{align*}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\&\begin{multlined}\lesssim\left(1+\frac{R_1}{\ell+R^+}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^++R_1}(0)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2X\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel \right)^{\tilde{q}}\right]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel,.\end{multlined}\end{align*}
Since  $X\mapsto\mathbb{E}[|X|^{\frac{q}{2}}]^{\frac{1}{\frac{q}{2}}}$is a norm we have by Jensen's inequality and the stationarity of $\nabla W_1$
\begin{equation}\begin{aligned}\label{eq:statw1}&\frac{1}{\left(\ell+R^+\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{\ell+R^++R_1}(0)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\&\leq\frac{1}{\left(\ell+R^+\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(0)}\int_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\&\leq\frac{1}{\left(\ell+R^+\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}}\int_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(0)}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{R_1}(0)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{R_1}(0)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\end{aligned}\end{equation}
Since $X\mapsto\mathbb{E}[|X|^{\tilde{q}}]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}$ is a norm we get by Jensen's inequality
\begin{align*}&\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2X\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel \right)^{\tilde{q}}\right]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\\&\leq\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\fint_{\square_{R_1}(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel)}\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{2\tilde{q}}|X|^{\tilde{q}} \right]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\\&=\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_0^h\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{2\tilde{q}}|X|^{\tilde{q}} \right]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel.\end{align*}In the last inequality we simplified the spatial averages in the right-hand side thanks to \eqref{eq:aver}. Moreover since the supremum is computed over positive $X$ we can reformulate it as follows
\begin{align*}\sup_{\|X\|_{L^{\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_0^h\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^{2\tilde{q}}|X|^{\tilde{q}} \right]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel&=\sup_{\|Y\|_{L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\int_0^h\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega Y|^{2\tilde{q}} \right]^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\\&=\sup_{\|Y\|_{L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}=1}\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega Y\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times (0,h), L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega))}^2\end{align*}
Appealing to Theorem \ref{thm:aux} we get
\begin{align*}\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega Y\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times (0,h), L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega))}=\|\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\nabla u_{Y\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times (0,h), L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega))}&\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{g} Y\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+, L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega))}\\&=\|Y\|_{L^{2\tilde{q}}(\Omega)}\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d}.\end{align*}
Finally we proved that
\begin{equation}\begin{multlined}\label{eq:f2}
  \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)\left(\fint_{\square_{\ell+R^+}(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel)}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla u_{\boldsymbol{g}}^\omega|^2\right)\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\\lesssim\|\boldsymbol{g}\|_{L^2(\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+)^d}^2\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\square_{R_1}}\int_0^h\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}.\end{multlined}\end{equation}We inject \eqref{eq:f2} into \eqref{eq:f1} and conclude thanks to the weight $\pi$ with super-algebraic decay.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Let $R\geq R^+$ and $L\geq R+H$. Take $\chi$ a smooth cut-off function such that $0\leq\chi\leq 1$, $\chi|_{\square_R\times(0,L)}\equiv1$, $\textrm{supp}\chi\subset\square_{2R}\times (0,2L)$ and $\vspace{0.1cm}\left|\nabla \chi(\boldsymbol{x})\right|\lesssim \displaystyle\frac{1}{R}$ for all $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}$. We test \eqref{eq:w10} with $v\coloneqq \chi W_1$. We obtain 
\begin{equation*}
  \int_{\square_R}\int_0^{L}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\leq-\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L}\nabla W_1^\omega\cdot W_1^\omega\nabla \chi+\int_{\square_{2R}\cap\Sigma_H}\chi W_1^\omega.
\end{equation*}
Using Young's inequality with $K\geq 1$ we get
\begin{multline*}
  \int_{\square_R}\int_0^{L}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2\lesssim\frac{1}{R^2}\frac{K^2}{2}\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|W_1^\omega|^2+\frac{1}{2K^2}\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\\+\frac{1}{2K^2}\int_{\square_{2R}\cap\Sigma_H}|W_1^\omega|^2+K^2R^{d-1}.
\end{multline*}
Next we apply the trace Theorem to the third term of the right-hand side 
  \begin{multline}\label{eq:init}
    \int_{\square_{R}}\int_0^{L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\lesssim K^2\left(R^{d-1}+\frac{1}{R^2}\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|W_1^\omega|^2\right)\\+\frac{1}{K^2}\left(\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\right).
  \end{multline}
  Recall that under Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty} Poincar\'e's inequality holds in boxes larger than $R^+$ with a constant linear in $R^+$. We decompose the second term of the right-hand side as follows
\begin{align*}
  \int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|W_1^\omega|^2&\leq \int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|W_1^\omega-\text{\large $\chi$}_r*W_1^\omega|^{2}+\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r*W_1^\omega|^{2}.\end{align*} 
where $\text{\large $\chi$}_r(\boldsymbol{x})\coloneqq r^{-d}\text{\large $\chi$}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}\right)$. On one hand, we obtain by Poincar\'e's inequality under Hypothesis \nameref{hyp:Linfty}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cnw}
\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r*W_1^\omega|^{2}\lesssim (R^+)^2\int_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r*\nabla W_1^\omega|^{2}.
\end{equation}
On the other hand, we write
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\left(W_1^\omega-\text{\large $\chi$}_r*W_1^\omega\right)(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel,x_d)=\int_0^r\int_{\square_r}\int_0^1\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\text{\large $\chi$}_r(\boldsymbol{y})\nabla W_1^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}+t\boldsymbol{y})\cdot \boldsymbol{y}\mathrm{d} t\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel\mathrm{d} y_d.
\end{equation*}
Using Jensen's inequality, we get
\begin{equation}\begin{aligned}\label{eq:nw} 
  \int_{\square_{2R}}\int_{0}^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|W_1^\omega-\text{\large $\chi$}_r*W_1^\omega|^{2}&\leq \int_{\square_{2R}}\int_{0}^{2L}\int_{0}^r\int_{\square_r}\int_0^1\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r(\boldsymbol{y})|^2|\nabla W_1^\omega(\boldsymbol{x}+t\boldsymbol{y})|^2\,|\boldsymbol{y}|^2\,\mathrm{d} t\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}
  \\&\lesssim r^2\int_{\square_{2R+r}}\int_{0}^{2L+r}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla W_1^\omega(\boldsymbol{x})|^2\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}. 
\end{aligned}\end{equation} 
Plugging back \eqref{eq:cnw} and \eqref{eq:nw} into \eqref{eq:init} we finally proved the following 
\begin{multline}\label{eq:interm}
  \fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{0}^{L}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\lesssim K^2\left(1+\fint_{\square_{2R}}\int_{0}^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r*\nabla W_1^\omega|^{2}\right)\\+\left(1+\frac{r}{2R}\right)^{d-1}\left(\frac{1}{K^2}+K^2 \frac{r^2}{R^2}\right)\fint_{\square_{2R+r}}\int_{0}^{2L+r} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2.
\end{multline}
Next we take the $L^{\frac{q}{2}}(\Omega)$ norm of both sides of \eqref{eq:interm} for $q\geq 2$. By stationarity and Jensen's inequality we have on one hand
\begin{align*}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{2R}}\int_{0}^{2L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r*\nabla W_1^\omega|^{2}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}&\leq\int_{0}^{2L}\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\text{\large $\chi$}_r*\nabla W_1^\omega|^{q}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\&\leq\int_0^{2L}\mathbb{E}\left[ \left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}^+}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\text{\large $\chi$}_r(\boldsymbol{y}_\shortparallel, x_d-y_d)\nabla W_1^\omega(\boldsymbol{y})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{y}\right|^{q}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\mathrm{d} x_d,\end{align*}
and on the other hand similarly as in \eqref{eq:statw1}\begin{gather}\label{eq:aver2}\left(1+\frac{r}{2R}\right)^{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{2R+r}}\int_{0}^{2L+r} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{0}^{2L+r} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}.\end{gather}
To conclude we want to absorb the last term of the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:interm} into the left-hand side. 
In order to do so we decompose the integral in $y_d$ in two parts: $(0,L)$ and $(L, 2L+r)$. 
Thanks to \eqref{eq:aver2} choosing $K\gg 1$ and $r={R}^\alpha$ for some $\alpha\in(0,1)$ so that $\frac{1}{K^2}+K^2\frac{r^2}{R^2}$ is small enough we can absorb $\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{0}^{L} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}$ into the left-hand side. 

Let us deal with the last term $\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{L}^{2L+r} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}$. We plug in $W_1^{\omega}$'s integral representation \eqref{eq:repint_W10} and use Jensen's inequality
\begin{align*}
  &\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{L}^{2L+r} \mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^2\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\lesssim\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{L}^{2L+r}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\mathbb{E}\left[|\nabla  W_1^\omega|^q\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\\
  &\lesssim\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{L}^{2L+r}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, x_d-L)|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_{\shortparallel}}|\varphi^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{w})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{w}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2,\end{align*}
where $\varphi\coloneqq W_1^\omega|_{\Sigma_{L'}}$ with $L>L'$ and $\partial_d\Gamma$ is defined in \eqref{eq:der_Gamma}. By the trace Theorem we deduce
\begin{equation*}\begin{multlined}
  \fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{0}^{2L+l}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, x_d-L)|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_0^{\boldsymbol{z}_{\shortparallel}}|\varphi^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{x}_\shortparallel-\boldsymbol{w})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{w}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2\\\lesssim\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{L}^{2L+l}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, x_d-L')|(2R)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{L'}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{w})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{w}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2,\end{multlined}\end{equation*}where the constant is proportional to $R^+L'$. We obtain finally
\begin{align*}
  &\fint_{\square_{R}}\int_{L}^{2L+l}\mathbb{1}_{D^\omega}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}|\nabla \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, x_d-L')|(2R)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{L'}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{w})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{w}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2\\&\lesssim(2R)^{d-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\fint_{\square_{2R}}\int_0^{L}|\nabla W_1^\omega|^2(\boldsymbol{w})\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{w}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\right]^{\frac{2}{q}}\int_L^{2L+l}\left|\int_{\square_R}|\nabla \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, x_d-L')|\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2.
\end{align*} 
We conclude once again by stationarity of $W_1$ and thanks to the integrability property of $\nabla \partial_d\Gamma$ that ensures that both in $2$ and $3$ dimensions
\begin{equation*}
  R^{d-1}\int_L^{2L+l}\left|\int_{\square_R}|\nabla \partial_d\Gamma(\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel, x_d-L')|\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{z}_\shortparallel\right|^2=o(R,L).\end{equation*}
